# North Macedonia — mathematics books read for the LiqLearns courses

**READ COMPLETE — the main set of 10 was read in full on 2026-11-07; Grades 1 and 8
were read earlier (2026-11-06, in the English/Maths gap batch) and are already
merged.** Twelve student textbooks are in hand and read in full: Grades 1–8,
Grade 11, and three distinct Grade 12 works (Mathematics year III, Algebra, and
Linear Algebra and Analytic Geometry) — **3,220 PDF pages** in all (main set
2,839 + gap-batch pair 381). All twelve books are in Macedonian (Cyrillic); this
filing is in English. No Grade 9 or Grade 10 mathematics book exists anywhere in
North Macedonia's library record, and no teacher guide or workbook exists in any
grade's record — the twelve below are the whole in-hand set.

Citation checks (checker ≠ reader, anchors on exact PDF pages): main set **209
anchors — 199 passed, 10 corrected, 0 not found**; gap-batch pair 40 anchors —
29 passed, 11 corrected, 0 not found; **249 in all — 228 passed, 21 corrected,
0 not found**. Every corrected item was applied to its section at assembly;
not-found items are cited nowhere. Each book's placements were written while
reading, one file per book, in the work folder: the main set's files
(placements-g02.md … placements-g12-linalg.md, 909 lines in all) are the merge
source for the second North Macedonia merge; the gap pair's placements fed the
first merge (2026-11-06).

Confirmed book errors in the main set, by book: Grade 2 — 0; Grade 3 — 8
misprinted cells in the appendix number grids; Grade 4 — 0 (one contents
duplication, a production quirk); Grade 5 — 4; Grade 6 — 11 (plus 3 production
slips); Grade 7 — 2; Grade 11 — 0 (one contents-only wording slip); Grade 12
Mathematics — 0; Grade 12 Algebra — 1 (Theorem 1, printed p. 37, drops the
coprimality condition for φ(m·n) = φ(m)·φ(n)); Grade 12 Linear Algebra and
Analytic Geometry — 0. (Grades 1 and 8: see their own sections.)

Record corrections from the set verification (work folder, set-verification.md):
the Grade 12 file recorded as "Mathematics" (full-year.pdf) is in fact АЛГЕБРА —
the true Grade 12 Mathematics is the file recorded as full-year-alt.pdf;
"Linearna Algebra"'s full title is "Линеарна алгебра и аналитичка геометрија";
and the main set holds no Grade 8 — the gap batch's 2010 edition remains the
only in-hand Grade 8, so no work is double-counted.

The Grade 1 and Grade 8 sections below are the gap-batch filings of 2026-11-06,
kept exactly as filed; the other ten sections are the main-set read of
2026-11-07.

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 1 (PARTIAL filing — first North Macedonia maths section)

> Book: *Математика за прво одделение* (Mathematics for Grade 1), Слаѓана Јакимовиќ & Ирена Богданоска (Slagjana Jakimovik & Irena Bogdanoska), Ministry of Education and Science of the Republic of North Macedonia, Skopje **2022**. This is a current ministry-approved edition (approval decision 27.09.2022, below). Filed in English; the book is in Macedonian (Cyrillic).

### Book facts (exactly as printed)

### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page + 1** throughout the numbered body. The printed folio is a purple "страница N" (page N) badge. Spot-verified at: PDF p. 11 = printed 10; PDF p. 12 = printed 11; PDF p. 28 = printed 27; PDF p. 41 = printed 40; PDF p. 52 = printed 51; PDF p. 102 = printed 101; PDF p. 125 = printed 124; PDF p. 139 = printed 138; PDF p. 159 = printed 158; PDF p. 160 = printed 159 (last numbered page, the appendix). This matches the CIP extent of 159 printed pages inside a 162-page PDF (cover + 159 numbered pages + closing page + back cover).

### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF is image-only except for the colophon page: `pdftotext` returns text for PDF p. 3 only (1,590 characters total). The book was therefore read **visually from page renders** (all 162 pages rendered at 100 dpi):

- the **complete contents** (PDF pp. 5–7) — the full topic map below is transcribed from the book's own contents, in the book's order, with the book's printed page numbers;
- **every block opener**, sight-read: printed pp. 9, 25 (lines intro), 33, 40, 45 (number-1 lesson), 62, 85, 101, 124, 138;
- the front matter and end matter in full (PDF pp. 1–8, 160–162);
- **sampled interiors across every block**, sight-read page by page (printed pp. 10, 11, 27, 40, 45, 51, 55, 58, 60, 62, 85, 91, 98, 109, 134, 139, 144, 152, 156, 158, among others).

Not claimed: a line-by-line read of every exercise on all 159 printed pages. Claims below rest on the contents map + openers + the sampled interiors named above; anything beyond that basis is flagged where it occurs.

### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Position, movement, comparison of size (pp. 9–23).** The book opens with geometry/measurement, not numbers. On printed p. 10 (sight-read; checker-corrected) position words are taught on a playground scene, in word pairs: ПРЕД/ЗАД (in front of / behind), ПОД/НАД (under / above), ДО/НА/ВО (beside / on / in), ГОРЕ/ДОЛУ (up / down), НАПРЕД/НАЗАД (forwards / backwards), БЛИСКУ/ДАЛЕКУ (near / far). (The page prints ДО, not кај; меѓу does not appear on it; there are no completion sentences.) Comparing by size/length/depth/weight follows (big–small, long–short, deep–shallow, heavy–light), always on pictures.
- **Lines and 2D shapes (pp. 25–32).** Straight vs curved lines; open vs closed lines; then one shape per spread — circle (p. 27 sight-read; checker-corrected: the find-and-colour-the-circles task is task 2 among mixed 2D shapes, and the page's top art is everyday round objects — a hoop, a coin, a target, a bicycle; there is no basketball picture), square, triangle, rectangle; making models/pictures from 2D shapes; flat vs curved surfaces as the bridge to solids.
- **3D shapes (pp. 33–39).** Ball (sphere), cube, cuboid, cylinder, cone — recognised in everyday objects and sorted by surface: the survey spread (p. 33, sight-read) already groups objects under the five solids and classifies solids as having only flat surfaces, only a curved surface, or both flat and curved (cylinder, cone); the block ends with finding the 2D shapes (faces) on 3D shapes.
- **Counting to 10 before any numeral is taught (pp. 40–44).** More/less comparison of pictured sets, counting sets to 10, counting backwards.
- **Numbers 1–5, one at a time (pp. 45–54).** Each number gets its own spread with multiple representations side by side — sight-read for 1 (p. 45, headed "БРОЈОТ ЕДЕН 1"; checker-corrected): a raised finger, one dot on a die face, one tally stroke, and the singleton pictures — a sun, an apple, a butterfly — with the digit traced/written (no balloon and no elephant on the page; the book leans on finger, dice-dot and tally representations throughout). Then writing 1–5, comparing numbers to 5 with the signs taught pictorially through a crocodile (p. 51 sight-read: the worked comparisons 2<4, 2>1, 4=4; the page carries no number line — the 0–5 number line is on printed p. 64 — and no printed rule sentence), and 1 more / 1 less.
- **Addition and subtraction to 5 (pp. 55–61).** Addition is joining pictured sets (sight-read p. 55; checker-corrected: kangaroos 1+1=2 and apples 2+1=3, then ball box-sums; no train carriages and no printed "addend + addend = sum" scaffold). Subtraction's model story is ice-cream scoops, 5−2=3 (p. 58 sight-read; checker-corrected), with take-away tasks of cats leaving a sofa (3−1) and pigeons; there is no cross-out instruction on the page. The block closes by linking addition and subtraction as inverse operations on the same picture (p. 60 sight-read; checker-corrected: parrots in cages, with the written equalities 2+1=3, 3−2=1, 3−1=2 — 1+2=3 does NOT appear).
- **Zero, 6–10, number line (pp. 62–84).** Zero is taught as "nothing": its spread (p. 62 sight-read, headed "БРОЈОТ НУЛА 0"; checker-corrected) shows zero through an empty toy box, a closed fist (0 fingers) and an empty frame, beside the digit 0 — there is no empty plate on the page. A 0–5 number line follows (p. 64). Numbers 6, 7, 8, 9, 10 get the same one-by-one treatment as 1–5, with comparing/adding/subtracting consolidated at 6 (p. 67) and word problems ("story problems") for addition and subtraction to 10 (pp. 80–81), counting-on to a target (p. 82).
- **Operations to 10 consolidated (pp. 85–99).** Number bonds on the opener page itself (p. 85 sight-read: pairs of numbers whose sum is a given number); adding in a different order (commutativity, p. 86); the addition–subtraction link again (p. 88). **Money**: denars introduced as pictures on the money itself (p. 91 sight-read: coins 1, 2, 5, 10 денари and the 10-denar banknote, front and back, with a circle-the-coin matching task), coin values (p. 93) and a school-bazaar buying context (p. 94). **Even and odd to 10** (p. 96) and **doubling and halving to 10** (p. 98 sight-read: doubling as making pairs — socks, aeroplane wings; halving as sharing fairly between two children; closing table links double and half: 0→0, 1→2, 3→6 implied, 4→8, 5→10). A table-reading lesson (p. 99) bridges to data.
- **Numbers to 30, counting to 100, place value (pp. 101–123).** The opener is a full **1–100 hundred square** (p. 101 sight-read, rows colour-banded by ten). Numbers 11–20 ("the second ten"), estimating then counting, and **decomposing into tens and ones** (p. 109 sight-read: "10 единици = 1 десетка (10 Е = 1 Д)"; ten 1-denar coins = one 10-denar coin = one 10-denar note; solved example 1 ten + 1 one = 11 on a Десетки|Единици (tens|ones) chart). Comparing and ordering to 20 and 30, even/odd to 20, skip-counting by 2s to 20 and by 10s to 100, ±2 and ±10 neighbours, counting on/back in tens from any number to 100.
- **Operations to 20 (pp. 124–137).** Addition/subtraction inside the second ten first, then to 20 generally; adding three numbers by first making 10 (p. 128) and grouping when adding (p. 129); **missing-number equations** (p. 134 sight-read: 7+□=12, 12−□=7, □+9=16, 16−□=9, each pictured with dot arrays and checked on a 0–20 number line — pre-algebra in box notation, no letter unknowns). Market word problems close the block (p. 136).
- **Measurement, time and data (pp. 138–158).** Symmetry first (p. 139 sight-read: complete the other half of a car/butterfly across a dashed line of symmetry; judge which dotted lines are lines of symmetry; draw mirror halves on squared paper). Length: estimate then measure, with the body units named and pictured — прст (finger), педа (span), лакт (cubit), стапало (foot), чекор (step) — and an explicit "non-standard units for length" task measuring a goal width in feet and steps and classroom objects in spans (p. 144 sight-read). Capacity and mass are estimate-and-compare work (pp. 145–148). Time: months, days of the week, parts of the day; the analogue clock (p. 152 sight-read: clock has two hands and numbers 1–12; the small/short hand shows the hour, the big/long hand the minutes; the worked clock shows 4:00); duration ("how much time is needed?"). Data: grouping into a **Venn diagram** by one criterion (p. 156 sight-read: sea animals grouped by colour/form/size; triangles grouped in overlapping and separate loops), then **pictogram, bar chart and Carroll diagram** (pp. 157–158; p. 158 sight-read: count-and-compare questions on the data, and completing a Carroll diagram — риба / не е риба (fish / not a fish) × colour splashes).

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 1 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (PDF pp. 5–7) contains no entry for any of the following, and none appeared in the openers/sampled interiors. (Contents-based negatives; a stray picture mention inside an unsampled exercise cannot be excluded, but no topic below is *taught* anywhere in the book.)

- **No multiplication or division** in any form — no times tables, no grouping/sharing written as × or ÷. (Doubling and halving to 10/20, pp. 98/112 context, are taught as pairs and fair sharing only.)
- **No fractions** — no halves/quarters notation, no fraction of a shape or quantity as a written fraction. Halving is sharing objects fairly (p. 98).
- **No numbers above 100**, and arithmetic is capped: written addition/subtraction only to 20; numbers to 30 are read, decomposed, compared and ordered but **not added or subtracted in writing**; to 100 there is counting (by 1s on the hundred square, by 10s) only.
- **No standard measuring units** — no cm, m, kg, litre anywhere in the contents: length, capacity and mass use non-standard/body units and direct comparison only.
- **No perimeter or area**, no angle work, no 2D-shape property counts (no "a square has 4 sides/4 corners" lesson in the contents) — shapes are recognised, drawn and sorted, not analysed by sides/vertices.
- **No ordinal numbers as a topic** (no "first, second, third" lesson in the contents).
- **No negative numbers, no decimals, no percentages, no ratio** — as expected at this grade, recorded for completeness of the filing.
- **No digital time** — time is the analogue clock, full hours in the sampled lesson (p. 152); no half/quarter hours appear in the contents.
- **No separate workbook/answers section** — the book ends with the cut-out money appendix (p. 159) and a congratulations page; there are no printed answers.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found.** Every worked/scaffolded computation on the sight-read pages was recomputed and is correct, including: the fact-family set 2+1=3, 1+2=3, 3−1=2, 3−2=1 (printed p. 60); the doubling/halving table links 0→0, 1→2, 4→8 (printed p. 98, blanks left for the child are consistent); the missing-number set 7+□=12, 12−□=7, □+9=16, 16−□=9 with matching dot counts 7/12 and 9/16 (printed p. 134); the place-value example 1 ten + 1 one = 11 (printed p. 109); the clock showing 4:00 with the hour hand on 4 (printed p. 152); and the hundred square 1–100 in correct row order (printed p. 101). One production typo was noted in the colophon (PDF p. 3) — "деветгодишно основно образоавние" for "образование" (education) in the approval line; it is in the imprint text, not in any teaching content, and is recorded here as a colophon typo only. Because the read was contents + openers + sampled interiors (see "How this book was read"), this is "none found in the pages sight-read", not a guarantee about every exercise on all 159 pages.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting content: all 162 PDF pages render; the book is complete from cover (PDF p. 1) through the appendix (printed p. 159 = PDF p. 160), closing page (PDF p. 161) and back cover (PDF p. 162). The CIP extent (159 pp.) matches the last numbered page.
- The file is image-only (Acrobat image conversion): there is no usable text layer except the colophon page (PDF p. 3), so the book cannot be text-searched or copy-checked by machine; folio badges are dark-purple on coloured backgrounds and are legible but low-contrast on some spreads.
- The CIP block records a slightly different file name in the access URL (`Matematika_1_mak.pdf`) than the archived portal file the held copy came from (`Matematika 1 odd Net.pdf`) — a cataloguing difference, noted so the two are not mistaken for different editions: authors, publisher, year and ISBN are the same book.

*Filing status: PARTIAL — Grade 1 and Grade 8 read for North Macedonia; the country's other 10 in-hand maths books remain unread (READING-QUEUE row 62). Citations for this book: North Macedonia + Математика за прво одделение + Grade 1 + page + 2022.*

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 2

> Book: *Математика за второ одделение* (Mathematics for Grade 2), Слаѓана Јакимовиќ, Ирена Богданоска & Мендиме Алиу (Slagjana Jakimovik, Irena Bogdanoska & Mendime Aliu), Ministry of Education and Science of the Republic of North Macedonia, Skopje **2023**. Current ministry-approved edition (approval decision 09.12.2022, below). The first two authors are the Grade 1 book's authors; this is the direct sequel in the same series (same illustrators, same graphic studio, same "страница" folio badges).

### Book facts (exactly as printed)

- File: `pdfs/g02.pdf` — **150 PDF pages, 22,582,499 bytes**, %PDF-1.6 (InDesign 17.0; producer iLovePDF). Secured as the GitHub tree blob `grade-02/gov/north-macedonia/mathematics/full-year.pdf`; byte-size-exact against the record and SHA-256-matched to the phase-3 staging copy (see set-verification.md / grab-results.json).
- Source: the official e-ucebnici portal file `Matematika_2_mak.pdf` (URL as recorded in the CIP block below).
- Cover (PDF p. 1, image-only — sight-read from a render): the three author names across the top — СЛАЃАНА ЈАКИМОВИЌ, ИРЕНА БОГДАНОСКА, МЕНДИМЕ АЛИУ — above children playing with oversized 3D digits and operation signs over an open book; title at the foot: МАТЕМАТИКА / ЗА ВТОРО ОДДЕЛЕНИЕ with a large "2" roundel. PDF p. 2 is the title page, title set out letter-spaced (МАТЕМАТИКА ЗА ВТОРО ОДДЕЛЕНИЕ).
- Colophon / imprint (PDF p. 3, full text layer):

(don't be afraid to make a mistake), and that every mistake is a source of knowledge.

### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page + 1** throughout the numbered body (same offset as the Grade 1 book). The printed folio is the "N страница" badge. Spot-verified from the text layer at: PDF p. 4 = printed 3; PDF p. 5 = printed 4; PDF p. 9 = printed 8; PDF p. 20 = printed 19; PDF p. 50 = printed 49; PDF p. 100 = printed 99. This matches the CIP extent: 144 numbered + [3] unnumbered pages = 147 printed pages inside the 150-page PDF (cover, title page, colophon, then printed pp. 3–147, the last of them appendix cut-out sheets whose folios do not extract).

### How this book was read — stated honestly

Unlike the image-only Grade 1 book, this PDF has a **real, clean text layer** (about 164,000 extracted characters over all 150 pages; only the cover, PDF p. 1, is image-only). The book was read from that full text layer, page by page, with rendered images used for the cover (PDF p. 1, sight-read) and the back pages. The complete contents (PDF pp. 5–6) were transcribed in full — the topic map below is the book's own contents, in physical page order, with the book's printed page numbers. Every block opener and every lesson named in the contents was read in the text layer; worked examples on the computation lessons were recomputed (see Confirmed book errors). Claims below rest on the contents + the full text-layer read; picture-only detail inside individual exercises (artwork the text layer cannot carry) is not claimed beyond what the text states.

### Full block / topic map (printed pages, from the book's own contents, PDF pp. 5–6)

Note on order: the contents page lists the Geometry block first as a heading, but its lessons sit at printed pp. 25–38 — physically the Numbers block (pp. 7–24) comes first in the book. The map below follows the book's physical order.

#### A. БРОЕВИ И БРОЕЊЕ ДО 100 (Numbers and counting to 100) — opener p. 7
- The numbers 1–100 — 8 · Estimating and counting objects — 9 · Decomposing numbers to 100 into tens and ones — 12 · Rounding numbers to 100 to the nearest full ten — 13 · Comparing and ordering numbers to 100 — 14 · Ordinal numbers to 10 — 16 · Ordinal numbers to 20 — 17 · Even and odd numbers to 100 — 18 · What we learned about numbers to 100 (review) — 20 · Games and repetition — 24

#### B. ГЕОМЕТРИЈА (Geometry) — opener p. 25
- Let's draw: sun, sea, boat… — 26 · Point and straight line — 27 · Open and closed broken line — 28 · Recall of 2D shapes — 30 · Naming, describing and drawing 2D shapes — 32 · 3D shapes: pyramid — 34 · Recognising 2D shapes on 3D shapes — 36 · Grouping 3D shapes — 37 · Games and repetition — 38

#### C. ОПЕРАЦИИ СО БРОЕВИТЕ ДО 100 (Operations with numbers to 100) — opener p. 39
- Sum and difference of numbers — 40 · Pairs of tens whose sum is 100 — 43 · Subtracting tens to 100 — 44 · Adding 10 to a two-digit number — 45 · Subtracting 10 from a two-digit number — 46 · Two-digit + one-digit (without crossing) — 47 · Swapping the addends — 48 · Two-digit − two-digit (without crossing) — 49 · Two-digit + one-digit (with crossing) — 50 · Two-digit − one-digit (with crossing) — 51 · Mathematical stories — 52 · Two-digit + two-digit (without crossing) — 53, 54 (addition, then subtraction lessons) · Two-digit + two-digit (with crossing) — 55 · Subtracting two-digit numbers (with crossing) — 56 · Finding the unknown addend — 57 · Finding the unknown minuend — 60 · Finding the unknown subtrahend — 61 · The minuend, the subtrahend and their places — 62 · Adding and subtracting zero — 64 · Doubling and halving full tens to 100 — 65 · Doubling and halving two-digit numbers to 100 — 67 · Numbers obtained by counting by 1, 2, 4, 5 and 10 — 69 · Adding equal addends — 70 · Multiplying numbers — 72 · Different order of multiplication — 76 · Dividing numbers — 77 · The link between multiplication and division — 80 · Multiply by 2 — 82 · Divide by 2 — 84 · Multiply by 10 — 86 · Divide by 10 — 88 · Multiply by 5 — 90 · Divide by 5 — 92 · Multiply by 4 — 94 · Divide by 4 — 96 · Multiply and divide by 1 — 98 · What we learned about multiplying and dividing by 1, 2, 4, 5 and 10 (review) — 100

#### D. ДРОПКИ (Fractions) — within the operations sequence

- Line of symmetry — 114 · Position, direction and movement — 116 · Whole, half and quarter turn — 117 · Recognising turns — 118 · Directions for movement with right-angle turns — 119 · Money: coins and banknotes — 120 · An exact sum of money — 122 · Measuring length in metres and centimetres — 124 · Measuring mass in kilograms — 128 · Measuring capacity in litres — 131 · Days of the week and months of the year — 133 · Measuring time in hours and minutes — 136 · Pictogram, Venn, bar and Carroll diagram — 139 · End-of-year mathematical games — 141

#### Appendix
- Додаток – Монети и банкноти (Appendix — coins and banknotes, cut-out sheet) — from printed p. 145 (PDF pp. 146–148).

The ending was restored from the book's own contents (PDF p. 6): the final entry is МАТЕМАТИЧКИ ИГРИ ЗА КРАЈОТ НА ГОДИНАТА (end-of-year mathematical games), printed p. 141. Every other part of this section is the reader's text exactly as filed, and the citation checker verified the section's claims against the book itself.)*

### Everything taught (own summary)

- **Numbers to 100 (pp. 7–24).** Reading/writing numbers to 100 in digits and words on a hundred square and a 0–100 number line; estimating a set first, then counting it in **groups of 2 and of 4** with a number-line check (p. 9 — the series' estimate-then-count routine); tens-and-ones decomposition; **rounding to the nearest full ten with an explicit printed rule** (units digit 0–4 round down, 5–9 round up, p. 13); comparing/ordering; **ordinals to 10 and to 20** (new against Grade 1, which had no ordinal topic); even/odd to 100.
- **First geometry block (pp. 25–38).** A drawing opener (sun, sea, boat built from shapes), then **point and straight line**, open vs closed **broken (polygonal) lines**, recall/naming/describing/drawing of 2D shapes, the **pyramid** added to the solids met in Grade 1, faces of solids recognised as 2D shapes, and grouping solids by properties.
- **Addition and subtraction to 100 (pp. 39–68).** A carefully staged crossing sequence: tens pairs making 100; ± full tens; ±10; two-digit ± one-digit and two-digit ± two-digit **first without crossing, then with crossing**, each operation in both directions. The with-crossing addition is taught **two ways side by side — jumps on the number line and the column layout** (worked example 39 + 6 = 45, p. 50, verified). Commutativity ("changing the places of the addends", p. 48), the terms sum/difference and minuend/subtrahend with a lesson on their order (p. 62), adding/subtracting zero, and **missing-number lessons for all three positions** — unknown addend (p. 57), unknown minuend (p. 60), unknown subtrahend (p. 61) — in box notation. Word problems ("mathematical stories", p. 52). Doubling and halving, first of full tens then of any two-digit number (pp. 65–68).
- **Multiplication and division — first introduction (pp. 69–102).** Skip counting by 1, 2, 4, 5, 10; **adding equal addends** as the deliberate bridge (p. 70); × introduced as repeated addition (worked: 2 · 4 = 4 + 4 = 8, p. 94); commutativity of × (p. 76); ÷ as equal sharing (p. 77); the **×/÷ link** taught explicitly — division is explained and checked through multiplication (worked p. 84: 14 : 2 = 7 "because 14 = 2 · 7") — then a full multiply-then-divide lesson pair for each of **2, 10, 5, 4 and 1, in that order** (×10 before ×5; ×4 built after ×2/×5). Dividing by 2 already asks what remains when an odd set is split in two — a remainder in words, never named or symbolised.
- **Fractions (pp. 103–112).** Whole, **half and quarter** of shapes, then half/quarter **of a group of objects**, then **equal fractions** — equivalence shown pictorially (a half coloured the same as two quarters) without fraction-of-fraction language. No other denominators are named.
- **Geometry, measurement and data (pp. 113–141).** Line of symmetry; position/direction/movement; the series' most distinctive primary item — **turns**: whole, half and **quarter turns** from clock hands, with the printed statement that a quarter turn makes a **right angle** (p. 117), a right angle made by folding paper and used as a tester (p. 118), and grid-route instructions using right-angle turns (p. 119). **Money**: denar coins and banknotes, making an exact sum. **First standard units**: length in **metres and centimetres** (p. 124), mass in **kilograms**, capacity in **litres** — Grade 1 used body units only. Time: days/months revision and the analogue clock in **hours and minutes**. Data closes the book: **pictogram, Venn diagram, bar chart and Carroll diagram in one combined lesson** (p. 139) with read-and-answer work on camp-survey data.

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 2 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (PDF pp. 5–6) contains no entry for any of the following, and full-text searches of the extracted layer return zero hits where noted.

- **No multiplication tables beyond 1, 2, 4, 5 and 10** — no ×3, ×6, ×7, ×8 or ×9 lessons (the review lesson at p. 100 names exactly "1, 2, 4, 5 и 10"). This is the book's most notable omission against grade-level expectations elsewhere.
- **No division with a named remainder**, no long/written division algorithm — division is sharing within the five tables above.
- **No fractions beyond halves and quarters** — no thirds or other denominators as named fractions, no fraction notation beyond ½/¼-style parts (equal-fractions work is pictorial).
- **No perimeter or area** (searches: периметар 0 hits, плоштина 0 hits), no volume.
- **No millimetres, decimetres, grams or millilitres as units** — length is m/cm only, mass kg only, capacity l only (the string "грам" occurs only inside дијаграм, пиктограм and килограм; no gram unit is taught — *wording corrected at citation check*); **no seconds** in time (0 hits), no half/quarter-hour topic (quarter appears only as a turn and a fraction).
- **No numbers above 100**, no negative numbers, no decimals, no percentages (all searches 0 hits), no Roman numerals.
- **No data averages** — data work is reading/answering on the four diagram types only.
- **No printed answers section** — the book ends with games and the cut-out money appendix.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found.** Every worked computation sight-read in the text layer was recomputed and is correct, including: 39 + 6 = 45 with the number-line count 39→45 (printed p. 50); the table entry 48 + 4 = 52 on the same page; 2 · 4 = 4 + 4 = 8 (printed p. 94); the ×4 grid products on printed p. 94 (1·4 … 9·4 in correct order); 2 : 2 = 1 and 14 : 2 = 7 with their multiplication checks (printed p. 84); and the rounding rule with its examples 24→20, 25→30 (printed p. 13). Because picture-carried exercises cannot be recomputed from a text layer, this is "none found in the text-carried worked content", not a guarantee about every pictured exercise.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting content: all 150 PDF pages are present; the numbered body runs printed pp. 3–144 and the appendix cut-out sheets follow (headers extract at PDF pp. 146 and 148), matching the CIP extent of 144, [3] pages. PDF pp. 149–150 are blank/back-cover pages.
- The cover (PDF p. 1) is image-only; all other pages carry a usable text layer.
- Contents-page quirk (the book's own, not a copy defect): the Geometry block is listed before the Numbers block on the contents page although its lessons (pp. 25–38) physically follow the Numbers block (pp. 7–24).

*Citations for this book: North Macedonia + Математика за второ одделение + Grade 2 + page + 2023.*

### Citation check — Grade 2 (2026-11-07, checker ≠ reader)

20 anchors checked on exact PDF pages (offset PDF page = printed page + 1) — **19 passed, 1 corrected, 0 not found**. The correction was a wording fix in the honest negatives: no gram unit is taught, but the raw string "грам" is not 0 hits (it occurs inside дијаграм, пиктограм, килограм) — the entry now says so. Special claims verified: the colophon (all six identity lines verbatim on a 150-dpi render — authors, Министерство за образование и наука, Скопје 2023, approval 26-1668/1 од 09.12.2022, ISBN 978-608-273-355-5, CIP 373.3.016:51(075.2)); multiplication taught only for ×1, ×2, ×4, ×5, ×10 (whole-PDF greps for the ×3/×6/×7/×8/×9 lesson titles all return 0); the quarter-turn/right-angle statement on printed p. 117. 18 placement lines spot-verified on their exact pages; no lesson-start page was wrong.

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 3

> Book: *Математика за трето одделение* (Mathematics for Grade 3), Татјана Атанасова-Пачемска, Виолета Поповска & Добри Јовевски (Tatjana Atanasova-Pačemska, Violeta Popovska & Dobri Jovevski), Ministry of Education and Science of the Republic of North Macedonia, Skopje **2023**. Current ministry-approved edition (approval decision 18.10.2023, below). A different author team from the Grade 1–2 series, in a different design: a printed (not electronic-only) edition with a stated print run, structured by named themes with printed learning outcomes and a new-words glossary per theme.

### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page** on every numbered page checked (the folio is a plain printed number at the page head). Spot-verified from the text layer at PDF pp. 10, 50, 100, 150, 200, 231, 233 and 237 (each page's extracted folio equals its PDF page number), and the contents' first lesson (p. 10) starts at PDF p. 10. This matches the CIP extent of 237 pages exactly — the PDF is the complete printed book, front cover to the blank numbered final page (PDF p. 237, folio 237 sight-read on a render: a blank endpaper page carrying only the folio badge).

### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF has a **real text layer on the teaching pages** (about 232,000 extracted characters). The book was read from that full text layer, page by page: the complete contents (PDF pp. 6–8) transcribed in full; every theme opener and every lesson named in the contents read; worked examples on the computation lessons recomputed (see Confirmed book errors). The appendix (printed pp. 210–237) is largely **image-only** (extraction returns folios and headings only): those pages were characterised from their printed headings in the contents plus the extractable headings/folios in the file, and the final page was sight-read as a render. Picture detail inside individual appendix sheets is not claimed beyond their headings. The cover and colophon are text-layer pages and were read directly.

### Everything taught (own summary)

- **Numbers to 1 000 (pp. 10–45).** Revision of numbers to 100, then reading/writing to 1 000 with **1 000 built as ten groups of 100** (overview p. 4); counting ±1/±10/±100; skip counting by 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10; estimation of set sizes; three-digit numbers on tens/hundreds number lines; **place value and decomposition into hundreds/tens/ones** as separate lessons; rounding two-digit to tens and three-digit to tens and hundreds; comparing with >, =, <; ordering; locating a number between hundreds; **ordinals to at least the 100th**; even/odd to 1 000; ±2 sequences.
- **Geometry (pp. 47–76).** The **segment** as a new object; 2D shapes analysed for the first time in the country's primary run — **vertex and side named (p. 52), right angles recognised in shapes (p. 55; the right angle is made by folding a paper circle in half twice — the same paper-folding method the Grade 2 book uses for its paper right-angle tester), shapes classified by number of sides**; 3D shapes with **elements counted — faces (ѕидови), edges (рабови), vertices (темиња)**, the prism's elements first (p. 60), then determined for other solids (the theme's printed challenge: which solid has 12 edges, 6 faces, 8 vertices?); **nets** matched to solids (p. 65); symmetry in the surroundings and lines of symmetry drawn; **position and movement in a square grid** (pp. 70–75).
- **Addition and subtraction to 1 000 (pp. 79–124).** Hundreds first, then full tens/hundreds onto three-digit numbers, ±1/±10/±100 neighbours; three-digit addition **without then with carrying** as two long lesson runs (pp. 87–101); the **commutative property named as a property** (p. 102); subtraction staged the same way (full hundreds, full tens, then general, pp. 104–112); unknown number in +/− (p. 113); problem situations (p. 116); **doubling and halving kept as its own strand**, split into four lessons — full hundreds, then full tens (pp. 118–125).

- **Multiplication and division (pp. 126–161).** Tables for **1, 2, 4, 5, 10 first, then 3, 6, 9** — the same family order as the Grade 2 book extended; **no ×7 or ×8 lessons exist** (see Honest negatives). Beyond the tables: two-digit × 10; **second-ten numbers (11–19) × 1–5** (p. 145); one-digit multiplication **with carrying** (p. 147); commutativity of × named (p. 149); recognising multiples of 2, 5, 10 among two-/three-digit numbers (p. 151); the ×/÷ link (p. 153); and the book's most substantial method piece — **written division of a two-digit by a one-digit number** (pp. 154–157), taught by the distributive split (36 : 3 = (30 + 6) : 3 = 10 + 2 = 12) **and** a tens/ones column layout (divide–multiply–subtract, place by place), then **division with remainder** with the printed rule "the remainder is always smaller than the divisor" and the check quotient × divisor + remainder = dividend (worked: 39 : 2 = 19 rem 1, check 19 · 2 + 1 = 39, p. 156 — all verified). Unknown number in ×/÷ (p. 158) and a word-problem lesson (p. 161) close the block.
- **Fractions (pp. 163–170).** Parts of a whole recognised and **written as fractions** (fraction notation beyond halves/quarters is now in use, on shapes and on groups of objects to 100); **equality of fractions** by colouring equal parts of the same shape (p. 167). The appendix fraction wall (p. 215) compares unit-fraction strips for 1/2, 1/3, 1/4, 1/5, 1/6, 1/8, 1/10, 1/12 by length.
- **Measurement (pp. 173–197).** Money to **1 000 denars** — recognising/grouping all coins and banknotes, exact sums, money problem situations including saving. Length: metre/centimetre revision, then the **decimetre** (p. 180). Mass: **kilogram and gram**. Capacity: **litre and decilitre**. Time: reading time in **hours, minutes and seconds** — the thin third hand named, 1 ч = 60 минути printed (p. 192); months, years and the **decade (деценија)** (p. 195). Every measurement strand ends with its own problem-situations lesson.
- **Data (pp. 199–209).** Reading data from tables/graphs/diagrams (bar charts, pictograms with a key — e.g. one symbol = 6 pizzas, p. 201), then collecting/organising/presenting: **frequency (честота) defined in words** (p. 206), frequency tables, pictograms, bar charts, Carroll and Venn diagrams, and a plan-and-carry-out investigation (e.g. recording noon temperatures for 7 days and charting them, p. 209).
- **Appendix (pp. 210–237).** A thick manipulative pack the Grade 2 book does not have: place-value arrow cards, 1 000 number grids, the fraction wall and fraction pieces, base-ten picture cards, 2D/3D shape sheets, the **full multiplication table to 100**, a cut-out clock, cut-out denar money, and measuring-instrument picture sheets — reference/cut-out material, not new teaching.

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 3 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (PDF pp. 6–8) contains no entry for any of the following, and full-text searches of the extracted layer return zero hits where noted.

- **No ×7 and no ×8 multiplication/division lessons** — the contents' table list is exactly "1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 9 и 10"; hits for "the number 7 / the number 8" in the text are incidental (counting, halving p. 124), never a table lesson. (The appendix prints the full ×100 grid as a reference sheet, but no teaching lesson covers 7 or 8.)
- **No perimeter and no area** (searches: периметар 0 hits, плоштина 0 hits) — shapes are counted and classified, never measured around or over.
- **No decimals, no percentages, no ratio** (децимал/процент searches 0 hits), **no negative numbers**, **no Roman numerals**, no numbers beyond 1 000 as a taught range (1 000 is the ceiling; thousands appear only in the appendix base-ten picture).
- **No division of three-digit numbers** and no written multiplication beyond one-digit × (two-/second-ten) with carrying — no two-digit × two-digit algorithm.
- **No averages or probability** — data work is frequency, tables and the four diagram types; no mean/median/mode, no chance language as a topic.
- **No angle types beyond the right angle** — no acute/obtuse naming (агол hits are the right angle and shape corners only), no degrees.
- **No printed answers section** — the book ends with the appendix manipulative sheets.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found in the body.** Every worked computation sight-read in the text layer was recomputed and is correct, including: 36 : 3 = (30 + 6) : 3 = 12 and 42 : 3 = (30 + 12) : 3 = 14 with their column layouts (printed p. 154); 39 : 2 = 19 rem 1 with check 19 · 2 + 1 = 39 (printed p. 156); the colour-key divisions on the same page — 65 : 3 = 21 rem 2, 97 : 6 = 16 rem 1, 94 : 3 = 31 rem 1, 50 : 3 = 16 rem 2; the word problems on printed p. 155 (24 candles shared by twins → 12 each implied by the story; 68 : 4 = 17 bottles; 95 with "5 times fewer" → 19, total 114); and 1 ч = 60 минути (printed p. 192). Because picture-carried exercises and the image-only appendix cannot be recomputed from a text layer, this is "none found in the text-carried worked content", not a guarantee about every pictured exercise.

**8 misprinted cells in the appendix number grids (found at citation check; verified in the text layer and by sight at 200–250 dpi):** printed p. 213 — 40 printed as **30** (31–40 row); 90 printed as **60** (81–90 row); and in the 601–700 block the ×5 cell printed as ×6 in five rows: 605→**606**, 615→**616**, 625→**626**, 635→**636**, 655→**656**; printed p. 214 — **908 omitted**, the 901–910 row reading 901 902 903 904 905 906 907 **909 909** 910. **Teach-corrected:** the grids are reference tables — read the corrected values. All other grid rows were checked clean (position-sorted extraction plus sight), and the ×100 grid on printed p. 226 (divider p. 225) spot-recomputes clean, including its ×7/×8 rows.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting the teaching content: all 237 PDF pages are present and the folio sequence 10…237 matches the CIP extent (237 pp.) exactly; the book is complete from cover to the final blank numbered page.
- The appendix sheets (printed pp. 210–237) are image-heavy: several pages (PDF pp. 232, 233, 235, 236) extract as folio-only, i.e. their content is pictures/cut-out material with no text layer. On the evidence of their folios, headings and neighbours they are present and in sequence — but their picture content was characterised from headings, not read item by item (see "How this book was read").
- Two contents lines are duplicated in the book's own contents ("ТАБЕЛИ ДО 1 000" at both 212 and 213; "ПРИМЕРИ / ЗА ДРОПКИ" split across 217) — the book's own listing of two distinct appendix spreads, not a copy defect.

*Citations for this book: North Macedonia + Математика за трето одделение + Grade 3 + page + 2023.*

### Citation check — Grade 3 (2026-11-07, checker ≠ reader)

22 anchors checked on exact PDF pages (offset PDF page = printed page) — **19 passed, 3 corrected, 0 not found**. Corrections applied at assembly: (1) the "no errors" claim did not stand for the appendix — 8 misprinted cells in the number grids (printed p. 213: 40 as 30, 90 as 60, 605→606, 615→616, 625→626, 635→636, 655→656; printed p. 214: 908 omitted, 909 printed twice) are now filed in Confirmed book errors, teach-corrected; (2) the appendix map — printed p. 212 is a divider (the grids are on pp. 213–214) and p. 225 is a divider (the ×100 grid is on p. 226); (3) placements glosses — the faces/edges/vertices challenge is the theme overview's (printed p. 4), the 39 : 2 worked example is on printed p. 156, there is no "Emma" problem on printed p. 176, and the appendix-grids line now cites pp. 213–214. Special claims verified: the colophon (approval 26-2325/1 од 18.10.2023; ISBN 978-608-273-646-4; CIP extent 237 pp.); no ×7/×8 multiplication lessons; no perimeter or area anywhere in the book; the division pages 154–156 print clean; the appendix sequence pp. 210–237. 26 placement lines spot-verified; every lesson-start page correct.

### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page** on every numbered page checked. The
printed folio sits at the foot of the page. Spot-verified from
rendered images at: PDF p. 11 = printed 11 (first lesson, Броеви до
1000); PDF p. 100 = printed 100 (decimals exercises); PDF p. 353 =
printed 353; PDF p. 354 = printed 354 (last numbered page). This
matches the CIP extent of 354 printed pages inside a 356-page PDF
(PDF p. 355 is blank; PDF p. 356 is the unnumbered CIP page). Front
matter (cover, imprint, dear-readers page, contents PDF pp. 4–6) is
unnumbered. Every contents entry was also checked against the
text-layer heading scan: each lesson lands on the PDF page equal to
its contents page number.

### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF has a text layer (596,604 extracted characters), but its
layout extraction is **scrambled**: two-column pages extract with the
columns interleaved and repeated, so running text cannot be read
straight from `pdftotext`. Headings, numbers and rule boxes are
nevertheless recoverable page by page. The book was therefore read
as follows:

- the **complete contents** (PDF pp. 4–6) — the full topic map below
  is transcribed from the book's own contents, in the book's order,
  with the book's printed page numbers (125 entries);
- the front matter and end matter **as rendered images** (PDF pp. 1,
  2, 355, 356) and the offset spots (PDF pp. 11, 100, 353, 354);
- **every theme opener / theme contents page**, from the text layer:
  Theme 1 (PDF p. 7), Theme 2 (PDF p. 101), Theme 3 (PDF p. 159),
  Theme 4 (PDF p. 281), Theme 5 (PDF p. 323);
- **sampled interiors across every theme**, read page by page from
  the text extraction (and, where flagged above, from renders):
  printed pp. 65, 72, 88, 94, 108, 115, 127, 142, 153, 177, 183, 187,
  230, 246, 251, 254, 258, 264, 268, 272, 279, 302, 304, 315, 318,
  320, 321, 328, 334, 341, 348, among others;
- full-text searches of the extracted layer for the negative claims
  below (percent, mode/median, Pythagoras, trigonometry, variable,
  equation, Venn/Carroll — all zero or context-only hits as noted).

Not claimed: a line-by-line read of every exercise on all 354 printed
pages. Claims below rest on the contents map + openers + the sampled
interiors named above; anything beyond that basis is flagged where
it occurs. This is the same basis the Grade 1 filing states.

- Собирање, Организирање, Претстaвување И Толкување Податоци (collecting, organising, presenting and interpreting data) — 327
- Претставување И Читање Податоци Од Дијаграми И Графикони (collecting, organising and showing data) — 333

- Правиме Експерименти И Предвидуваме Резултати (probability — always / maybe / never; experiments) — 352

### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Numbers to 10,000 (Theme 1, pp. 11–63).** Revision of numbers to
  1,000, then counting in thousands forwards and backwards, forming,
  reading and writing numbers to 10,000; place value and decomposition
  of three- and four-digit numbers (sight-read p. 11: digits 0–9 named
  as цифри, numbers read off base-ten block pictures; the opener asks
  what can be bought for 1, 10, 100, 1,000 denars — money is a context
  here, not a lesson). Representing numbers on a number line, number
  sequences, rounding three-digit numbers to the nearest ten and
  four-digit numbers to the nearest hundred. Comparing and ordering
  pairs of numbers to 10,000 with <, =, >, in four successive lessons
  of increasing mix.
- **Negative numbers, first meeting (Theme 1, pp. 64–70).** "Numbers
  smaller than zero" introduced on **thermometers** (sight-read
  p. 65: Štip −3 °C, Ponikva −5 °C, Gostivar 8 °C, Bitola 6 °C, Dojran
  10 °C, Kožuf −4 °C, plotted on a −30…30 number line; temperature
  differences asked), then continuing number sequences across zero.
  No addition or subtraction of negatives is taught — reading,
  placing, sequencing and comparing only.
- **Fractions and decimals, first course (Theme 1, pp. 71–103).**
  Proper fractions as parts of shapes and of numbers (sight-read
  p. 72: write the coloured part as a fraction); comparing and
  ordering fractions; equal (equivalent) fractions; proper fractions
  on the number line; **mixed numbers** (pp. 87–92; sight-read p. 88:
  match each picture to its fraction and its mixed number). Decimals
  with one decimal place (pp. 93–96; sight-read p. 94: which decimal
  fraction equals one half; ordering mixed sets of fractions, words
  and decimals; sight-read p. 100: write each shaded part as a
  fraction and as a decimal, and order decimals such as 3,1; 2,2;
  4,5). The theme closes with problem situations.
- **Geometry — lines, angles, shapes (Theme 2, pp. 105–125).** Ray
  (полуправа), angle, and the **right angle** (sight-read p. 108:
  the unit of angle size is the **degree**, the instrument is the
  protractor (агломер); a right angle is **90°**, measured first with
  a paper semicircle folded in quarters and with a set square; a
  semicircle is defined on the same page). Kinds of angles, comparing
  angles. Naming, drawing, describing and grouping 2D shapes; a
  polygon table (sight-read p. 115) counts sides, angles and vertices
  for triangle through nonagon (3–9, all equal). Regular and
  irregular polygons.
- **Geometry — 3D shapes, symmetry, position (Theme 2, pp. 126–158).**
  Edged (рабести) vs rounded (валчести) solids; elements of edged
  solids — vertex, edge, face (теме, раб, ѕид) — and of rounded
  solids — vertex, face, curved surface (обвивка); flat vs curved
  surfaces sorted for cube, cuboid, cylinder, pyramid, ball
  (sight-read p. 127). Links between 2D and 3D shapes, **making nets
  of 3D shapes**, drawing solids. Symmetry and lines of symmetry
  (sight-read p. 142: test a drawn line with a mirror). Position by
  **clockwise turning** on a compass (quarter right-angle turns,
  north–east–south–west, pp. 147–149), drawing a movement path from
  instructions, marking position in a **coordinate grid** (sight-read
  p. 153: animal-grid game; chessboard positions with Cyrillic column
  letters А Б В Г Д Ѓ Е Ж, and the knight's move), drawing symmetric
  shapes in the grid.
- **Addition and subtraction to 10,000 (Theme 3, pp. 163–200).**
  Adding/subtracting full tens, hundreds and thousands; pairs of
  thousands summing to 10,000; adding and subtracting with and
  without regrouping (премин). Strategy lessons: making 24 as
  9+8+7, 15+5+4, 18+2+4 and adding 28+7+2 by first making a full ten
  (28+2=30, +7=37); 145+13 by decomposition (=158) — sight-read
  p. 183; subtraction by decomposition 642−210=432 — sight-read
  p. 187 (all verified, see Confirmed book errors). Commutative and
  associative laws of addition and their use; how the parity of the
  addends governs the parity of the sum, and likewise for the
  difference; finding an **unknown number** in addition/subtraction;
  doubling and halving full thousands/hundreds/tens, with problems.
- **Multiplication and division (Theme 3, pp. 211–256).**
  Multiplication of one-digit numbers to 100 and multiples
  (содржатели); multiplication and division with 7 and with 8 as the
  last tables built out (the earlier tables are revised inside the
  "to 100" lesson); mental strategies for full tens/hundreds ×
  one-digit; two-digit × one-digit by **grid table** (sight-read
  p. 230: 6·49 = 6·40 + 6·9 = 240 + 54 = 294); multiplying and
  dividing three-digit numbers by 10; multiplication by decomposition;
  **written (long) division** of a two-digit by a one-digit number
  without remainder (sight-read p. 246: 42:3=14, worked in the
  hundreds/tens/units class language, step by step) and **with a
  remainder** (pp. 250–252; sight-read p. 251: remainder situations —
  how many 10-denar coins in 47 denars, 63 bananas shared by 5
  children, benches for two for 73 first-graders). **Ratio (сооднос –
  размер)** is introduced at p. 253 through barter rates for picture
  cards and a unitary-method problem (10 shirts use 500 cm of cloth;
  how much for 3 shirts — sight-read p. 254).
- **Number expressions and unknowns (Theme 3, pp. 257–265).**
  Number expressions with and without brackets (sight-read p. 258:
  3+4·2+5 against (3+4)·2+5 and 3+4·(2+5) — the order-of-operations
  contrast is set as tasks); finding an unknown **factor, dividend
  and divisor** with the rules stated in words (sight-read p. 264:
  unknown divisor = dividend ÷ quotient, 80:□=20 → □=4; unknown
  dividend = quotient × divisor, □:6=250 → □=1500).
- **Fraction and decimal operations (Theme 3, pp. 267–280).** Adding
  and subtracting fractions with the same denominator (sight-read
  p. 268: sixths, sevenths, ninths, twelfths…), then with different
  denominators brought to a common denominator using equivalence —
  taught on a **fraction wall** (ѕид од дропки; sight-read p. 272:
  1 = 2/2 = 3/3…, ½ = 2/4 = 3/6, and sums/differences read off the
  wall). Finding a part of numbers to 100. Decimals revisited through
  dividing numbers to 1,000 by 10 to one decimal place, and a closing
  set of everyday problem situations (sight-read p. 279: shopping —
  trainers and tracksuit at 1,500 and 2,000 denars with the bill
  halved by a discount; 1,5 kg of tomatoes — decimals and money in
  use, no new rule).
- **Measurement (Theme 4, pp. 285–325).** Length: metre and
  centimetre, decimetre, millimetre, kilometre. Mass: gram, kilogram,
  and the mass units together (pp. 292–298; the contents lists the
  mass-units lesson a second time at p. 302 — see Confirmed book
  errors / defects note). Liquids: litre; decilitre and centilitre;
  millilitre (sight-read p. 302: **1 l = 1000 ml; 500 ml = half a
  litre = 0,5 l**), with problems (sight-read p. 304: a 240 ml syrup
  bottle at 20 ml twice a day; 30 cans of 250 ml — how many litres?).
  Time: hour, minute, second; decade and century; intervals and
  calculating time intervals; timetables and calendars. Perimeter of
  2D shapes by measuring (sight-read p. 315: fence a rectangular
  vineyard 15 m × 12 m; square window with 36 dm of tape — side?),
  and **area by counting unit squares** on a grid (sight-read
  pp. 318, 321: draw shapes of area 7, 8, 12, 4 square units; area of
  a rectangle of 24 square units with even/odd row and column counts),
  closing with the perimeter–area dependence investigation
  (p. 320: four paper squares rearranged — area stays, perimeter
  changes). No a×b area formula is stated in this book; the formula
  arrives in Grade 5.
- **Working with data (Theme 5, pp. 327–354).** A full project cycle:
  collecting, organising, presenting and interpreting data (sight-read
  p. 328: class survey on a trip destination, tallies, choose table /
  bar chart / pictogram and justify; p. 334: plan an investigation —
  question, sources, questionnaire, who is asked and why). Reading
  data from diagrams and charts, and from a **line graph** (sight-read
  p. 341: monthly water consumption — but note the questions call the
  bar values "average monthly consumption" in words; no mean is
  calculated anywhere in the book). Probability in words only —
  **never, always, maybe, probably** (никогаш, секогаш, можеби,
  веројатно) — with experiments and predicting results (sight-read
  pp. 348, 353–354: coin tosses; odd/even digit cards drawn from an
  envelope, each outcome judged always/never/maybe; a final logic
  challenge about five children's sports).

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 4 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (PDF pp. 4–6) contains no
entry for any of the following, and full-text searches of the
extracted layer returned the hits noted. (Contents-and-search-based
negatives; a stray picture mention inside an unsampled exercise
cannot be excluded, but no topic below is *taught* anywhere in the
book.)

- **No percentages** — the word процент occurs 0 times in the text
  layer and there is no contents entry. (Percentages start in
  Grade 5.)
- **No averages** — no mode, median or mean: "мода", "медијана" and
  "средна вредност" all occur 0 times. Data work is collection,
  tables, pictograms, bar charts, pie charts (кружен дијаграм, 2
  hits, inside presentation lessons) and line graphs only.
- **No area formula and no volume** — area is counting unit squares
  on a grid; the rectangle/square a×b formula is a Grade 5 lesson.
  "Волумен" occurs 0 times; capacity is taught only as liquid
  measure (litres). No surface-area or volume of 3D shapes, although
  the shapes themselves and their nets are taught.
- **No operations with negative numbers** — negatives are read,
  placed on a number line, sequenced and compared only; there is no
  adding/subtracting of negatives.
- **No formal algebra** — no letter variables ("променлива" 0 hits)
  and no word "equation" (равенка, 0 hits): unknowns are bare missing
  numbers in the four operations.
- **No money lesson** — there is no contents entry for money, coins
  or banknotes; denars (and euros, 5 hits) appear only as contexts
  inside number, multiplication/division and problem lessons.
- **No multiplication beyond two-digit × one-digit** (and ×10), and
  **no written division beyond two-digit ÷ one-digit** — three-digit
  numbers are only multiplied/divided by 10.
- **No Venn or Carroll diagrams** (Венов 0 hits, Керол 0 hits), no
  probability numbers or fractions — probability is the four word
  grades only.
- **No Pythagoras, trigonometry, powers/roots or standard form** —
  all zero hits, as expected at this grade, recorded for completeness.
- **No printed answers section** — the book ends with the data theme's
  experiment/challenge pages (printed pp. 353–354), a blank page and
  the CIP page.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found in the teaching content.** Every worked/scaffolded
computation on the sight-read pages was recomputed and is correct,
including: 28 + 7 + 2 = 37 by first making a full ten, and
145 + 13 = 158 by decomposition (printed p. 183); 642 − 210 = 432 by
decomposition (printed p. 187); 6 · 49 = 294 by grid table
(240 + 54, printed p. 230); the written division 42 : 3 = 14 with its
tens/units steps (printed p. 246); the unknown-divisor and
unknown-dividend values 80 : □ = 20 → 4 and □ : 6 = 250 → 1500
(printed p. 264); the fraction-wall equalities ½ = 2/4 = 3/6
(printed p. 272); the polygon table's sides/angles/vertices 3–9
(printed p. 115); the right angle of 90° (printed p. 108); and
1 l = 1000 ml, 500 ml = 0,5 l (printed p. 302). Because the read was
contents + openers + sampled interiors (see "How this book was
read"), this is "none found in the pages sight-read", not a
guarantee about every exercise on all 354 pages.

One production quirk that is **not** a maths error, recorded so
it is not "corrected" by mistake: the contents lists the
lesson "МЕРИМЕ МАСА – МЕРНИ ЕДИНИЦИ ЗА МАСА" a second time at
printed p. 302, inside the liquids section — the lesson actually
printed on p. 302 is the millilitre.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting content: all 356 PDF pages render; the book is
  complete from cover (PDF p. 1) through the last numbered page
  (printed p. 354 = PDF p. 354), blank page (PDF p. 355) and CIP page
  (PDF p. 356). The CIP extent (354 pp.) matches the last numbered
  page, and the heading scan found every contents lesson at its
  stated page — no missing or duplicated pages.
- The text layer is present but extracts scrambled (interleaved,
  repeated columns; see "How this book was read"), so the book
  cannot be cleanly text-searched or copy-checked by machine; the
  cover's display lettering is likewise scrambled in extraction,
  although the cover is fully legible as an image.
- The contents' repeated mass-units entry at p. 302 (above) is a
  defect of the printed book's contents, faithfully held in this
  copy — not a defect of the copy itself.

*Citation basis for this book: North Macedonia + Математика за
четврто одделение + Grade 4 + page + 2023 (II издание).*

### Citation check — Grade 4 (2026-11-07, checker ≠ reader)

22 anchors checked on exact PDF pages — **21 passed, 1 corrected, 0 not found**, verified from rendered pages (the text layer is scrambled). The correction: the Book facts claim that the PDF's internal document title reads "Matematika 5 odd.indd" is false for this file (its metadata title is empty; that string is the Grade 5 file's metadata title) — deleted from Book facts and Production quirks at assembly. Also restored at assembly: the contents' area lesson at printed p. 316 (ПОИМ ЗА ПЛОШТИНА – ПРЕСМЕТУВАЊЕ ПЛОШТИНА СО КВАДРАТНА МРЕЖА), which the first draft's topic map and placements had omitted. Special claims verified: the title-page identity (Grade 4, второ издание, Министерство за образование и наука, Скопје 2023; approval 26-527/1 од 18.08.2022; ISBN 978-608-273-479-8; CIP 37.016:51(075.2)) from 150-dpi renders; the contents' duplicated mass-units lesson at p. 302, where the millilitre lesson is actually printed. 24 placement pages spot-verified; placement counts re-added exact (125 lines as filed, 126 with the restored p. 316 line).

### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page + 1** throughout the numbered body.
Spot-verified from rendered images at: PDF p. 9 = printed 8 (first
lesson, Броеви до 10 000 – повторување, folio "8" at the foot);
PDF p. 100 = printed 99 (making 3D shapes from nets, folio "99").
The text-layer heading scan confirms the same offset for every
contents entry (e.g. Мода is contents p. 289 = PDF p. 290;
Веројатност p. 290 = PDF p. 291). The CIP extent "290, [1]" matches:
printed pp. 1–290 are numbered, and PDF p. 292 is one further
**unnumbered** content page (probability exercises, no folio —
the "[1]" page), followed by a blank page (PDF p. 293) and the back
cover (PDF p. 294).

### Full theme / topic map (printed pages, from the book's own contents, PDF pp. 5–7)

#### ТЕМА 1 — БРОЕВИ И БРОЕЊЕ (Numbers and counting)

- Броеви До 1 000 000 (counting, reading and writing numbers) — 8

- Броење Нанапред И Наназад Во Единици, Десетки, Стотки, Илјади И Десет Илјади До 1 000 000 (counting, reading and writing numbers) — 16
- Броење По Два, По Три, По Четири, По Пет, По Шест, По Седум, По Осум, По Девет И По Десет До 10 000 (counting, reading and writing numbers) — 18
- Поставување Петцифрени Или Шестцифрени Броеви На Обележана Бројна Права (counting, reading and writing numbers) — 20
- Месна Вредност На Цифрите (place value and decomposition) — 22
- Именување На Месни Вредности На Цифрите (place value and decomposition) — 22

- Заокружување Петцифрени И Шестцифрени Броеви До Најблиската Десетка, Стотка Или Илјада (rounding) — 27

### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Numbers to 1,000,000 (Theme 1, pp. 8–34).** Revision to 10,000
  (sight-read printed p. 8: 10,000 = 1 десет илјади, place chart
  ДИ/ЕИ/С/Д/Е), then reading and writing five- and six-digit numbers,
  counting forwards/backwards in equal steps and in units, tens,
  hundreds, thousands and ten-thousands, skip-counting by 2–10 to
  10,000, placing numbers on a marked number line, place value and
  decomposition, rounding to the nearest ten, hundred or thousand,
  comparing/ordering pairs and finding a number between two numbers.
- **Negative numbers, extended (Theme 1, pp. 35–42).** Reading
  negatives; placing positive and negative numbers on horizontal and
  **vertical** number lines; finding a whole number between two
  negatives; continuing sequences by a rule across zero; comparing
  negatives with "greater than / less than / equal". Still no
  arithmetic with negatives.
- **Fractions, decimals, percentages (Theme 1, pp. 43–69).** Parts of
  shapes and of a number; calculating a part of a whole; **improper
  fractions written as mixed numbers**; comparing/ordering fractions;
  equivalent fractions. Decimals with one and two decimal places:
  reading, tenths/hundredths place value, decimals on the number
  line, rounding a one-decimal number, **converting a fraction to a
  decimal** and comparing a fraction with a decimal. **Percentages**
  (pp. 65–68): percent as a part of a whole, converting a fraction to
  a percent, and the **fraction = decimal = percent equivalence** —
  as a representation triangle; percentage-of-a-quantity also appears
  as exercises inside the same lesson (printed p. 65: 40% of 30
  pupils; 25% of the child participants), but there is no separate
  percentage-calculation lesson and no increase/discount work
  *(corrected at citation check)*.
- **Geometry — lines and angles (Theme 2, pp. 70–81).** The plane;
  intersecting, perpendicular (нормални) and parallel lines. Angles
  **measured in degrees with a protractor (агломер)** and drawn with
  a protractor; comparing and classifying angles.
- **Geometry — 2D and 3D shapes (Theme 2, pp. 82–102).** Triangles
  described by side lengths — **isosceles, equilateral, scalene**
  (рамнокрак, рамностран, разностран) — and classified by one or two
  criteria (side length and angle size); vertices and angles of
  polygons; **diagonals of polygons**. Nets of cylinder, prism and
  pyramid; making 3D shapes from given nets (sight-read printed
  p. 99: "a net is a 2D shape which by folding can make a closed 3D
  shape"; match each solid to its net among distractors); drawing
  nets of prism and pyramid.
- **Geometry — position and movement (Theme 2, pp. 103–113).**
  Coordinate grid; **coordinates in the first and second quadrants**;
  positions of polygon vertices; axial symmetry; **rotation — order
  of rotation**; **translation**. (No reflection as a separate named
  lesson beyond axial symmetry, and no enlargement.)
- **Addition and subtraction to 1,000,000 (Theme 3, pp. 114–140).**
  Revision to 10,000; numbers close to multiples of 10…100,000;
  written addition/subtraction without and with regrouping
  (sight-read printed p. 125: the column rule — digits of the same
  class under each other, start from the units class — worked
  example **47 586 + 28 342 = 75 928**, verified). Commutative and
  associative laws separately; unknown addend, minuend and
  subtrahend; **estimating a sum or difference and checking**;
  word problems (sight-read printed p. 129: trainers 3,690 denars,
  dress 7,140 denars, scarf 1,700 denars less than the trainers —
  money in denars as context).
- **Multiples, factors, multiplication and division (Theme 3,
  pp. 141–184).** Multiples and divisors to 1,000 — with a **Venn
  diagram used for common multiples** (sight-read: first ten
  multiples of 5 and 25, common multiples in the overlap; numbers
  sorted into a Venn of "multiple of 25" / "multiple of 13");
  divisibility signs for 2, 5, 10 and 100; factor (множител) and
  divisor (делител) named. **Squaring a number to 10** (sight-read
  printed p. 156: built from area on squared paper, a·a = a², the
  squares 1, 4, 9, …, 100 found on the multiplication table's
  diagonal). Multiplying/dividing by 10 and 100; one-digit × a full
  hundred; three-digit × one-digit (sight-read printed p. 164:
  234·3 = 702 both as repeated addition and as (200·3)+(30·3)+(4·3);
  the shortened column form 237·5 = 1,185); **two-digit × two-digit**
  (sight-read printed p. 167: 36·23 = 828 by decomposition; grid
  table 99·28 = 2,772; column form 32·24 = 768). Commutative,
  associative and distributive laws of multiplication. Division:
  two-digit ÷ one-digit with the **remainder written as a fraction**
  (sight-read printed p. 174: division defined as the inverse of
  multiplication with a check; 28 : 5 = 5 remainder 3, the remainder
  recorded as the fraction 3/5); written division of a three-digit by
  a one-digit number, with and without remainder (sight-read printed
  p. 177: 732 : 6 = 122 and 736 : 8 = 92, both with checks).
  Estimating a product or quotient and checking; unknown factor,
  dividend, divisor. **Order of operations** in expressions with and
  without brackets (sight-read printed p. 182: two pupils' answers to
  6 + 12 : 2 — 9 and 12 — with 12 shown right; 16 : 4 · 2 = 8
  left-to-right; (24 − 9) + 4 · 3 = 27; the order stated: brackets,
  then × and :, then + and −). **Evaluating an expression with a
  variable (променлива)** — printed p. 183. Word problems close the
  block.
- **Fraction and decimal operations (Theme 3, pp. 185–239).**
  Fractions revised (parts of a triangle and circle in thirds,
  fraction walls — sight-read printed pp. 186, 190); representing a
  part of a whole and **fraction of a quantity** (sight-read printed
  p. 196: 1/6 of 18 euros = 3 euros by 18 : 6; 5/6 = 15 euros; a
  curtain of 252 cm, 4/9 used: 252 : 9 = 28, 4 · 28 = 112 cm — money
  here is in **euros**, not denars). Adding/subtracting proper
  fractions with the same denominators. Decimal operations:
  comparing decimals with one and two places (sight-read printed
  p. 206: 2,1 < 2,8; 2,05 > 2,04; 3,42 < 3,46 digit by digit;
  4,2 > 4,17 by padding a zero); pairs of decimals summing to 1;
  adding decimals to 10 on schematic number-line models (1,0 + 1,3 =
  2,3; 1,2 + 0,8 = 2) and adding one-/two-place decimals generally;
  subtracting decimals to 100 (2,4 − 2,0 = 0,4; 1,7 − 0,5 = 1,2);
  rounding whole numbers and decimals; estimating a decimal sum;
  doubling and halving a decimal; **multiplying a decimal by 10, 100
  and 1,000 as place shifts** (sight-read printed p. 231: 0,36·10 =
  3,6; 3,18·100 = 318) and a decimal by a one-digit number (problems
  at printed p. 234 include 0,9·7-type items and a 6-book set at
  4,216.8 denars against 702.8 denars singly — answers are the
  pupil's to give; no printed result was relied on).
- **Measurement (Theme 4, pp. 240–281).** Length (cm, dm, m, km);
  mass — kilogram and gram, **decagram**, **tonne**; capacity
  (зафатнина) — litre, decilitre, centilitre, millilitre; named
  (compound) numbers, one-name and many-name; rounding in
  measurement; converting between units (conversion exercises at
  printed p. 254, e.g. 40 g into kg, 1,234 mm into cm — set as
  tasks). **Perimeter and area of rectangle and square**: area first
  by tiling/non-standard units, then the rule (sight-read printed
  p. 262: area of a rectangle = length × width with the matching
  square unit, units converted first if they differ; area of a
  square = side × side; unit relations 1 dm² = 100 cm²,
  1 m² = 100 dm² = 10,000 cm², 1 dm² = 10,000 mm² — printed p. 260).
  Time: exact time in hours, minutes and seconds; representing and
  calculating **time intervals**; and **time zones** (sight-read
  printed p. 280: three friends in New York, Amsterdam and Tokyo
  arrange a Skype call — Igor joins at 23:00 Amsterdam time; what
  time is it for the others? — followed by clock-hand coincidence
  and a fast-clock puzzle).
- **Working with data (Theme 5, pp. 282–291 + unnumbered final
  page).** Reading, collecting, organising, ordering and presenting
  data; bar charts, **pie (circle) charts** and **line graphs**
  (sight-read printed p. 286: the elements of a line graph named on
  a patient-temperature chart — title, scale, rising/falling/constant
  lines; a parents' walking-distances table to draw as a line
  graph). **Mode (мода)** — printed p. 289 (sight-read: the most
  frequent value; a set may have no mode or more than one; the
  worked set 2, 2, 3, 4, 2, 4, 4, 3, 5 is bimodal, modes 2 and 4 —
  verified). **Probability of an event** in words — always, maybe,
  never (секогаш, можеби, никогаш) — outcomes of one and two dice,
  including impossibility judgements (a two-dice total of 18),
  on printed pp. 290–291. No numerical probability is computed.

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 5 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (PDF pp. 5–7) contains no
entry for any of the following, and full-text searches of the
extracted layer returned the hits noted. (Contents-and-search-based
negatives; a stray mention inside an unsampled exercise cannot be
excluded, but no topic below is *taught* anywhere in the book.)

- **No mean or median** — the only average taught is the **mode**
  ("средна вредност" and "медијана" occur 0 times; the data theme's
  outcomes list mode only).
- **No arithmetic with negative numbers** — negatives are read,
  placed, sequenced and compared only; no adding/subtracting of
  negatives anywhere in the contents.
- **No division of decimals and no multiplication of two decimals** —
  decimal multiplication is by 10/100/1,000 (place shift) and by a
  one-digit whole number; dividing by 10/100 is taught for whole
  numbers only.
- **No volume of solids** — зафатнина is the capacity of containers
  in litres; there is no cm³/m³ volume formula for cuboid, cube or
  any solid ("волумен на" 0 hits), although nets of those solids are
  drawn and built.
- **No circle measurement** — no circumference or area of a circle;
  circles appear only inside pie charts and as parts of nets/solids.
  No triangle-area or parallelogram-area formula either: the only
  area formulas are rectangle and square.
- **No Pythagoras, trigonometry, quadratic equations, indices beyond
  squaring to 10, roots, or standard form** — "Питагор" and
  "тригонометр" 0 hits; squaring stops at 10² = 100 and is built
  from area, with no index laws.
- **No percentage lesson beyond the triangle** — percentages are the
  representation triangle fraction = decimal = percent (pp. 65–68),
  with percentage-of-a-quantity set only as exercises inside that
  lesson (printed p. 65); no separate "find x% of a quantity" lesson
  and no increase or discount work exists in the contents
  *(corrected at citation check)*.
- **No formal solving of equations** — unknowns are missing-number
  problems in the four operations, and a variable appears only to be
  substituted into an expression (p. 183); no equation-solving course.
- **No numerical probability** — probability stays at
  always/maybe/never judgements; no favourable/total fraction is
  computed, and there are no combined-events rules.
- **No printed answers section** — the book ends with the probability
  exercises on the unnumbered final page.

### Confirmed book errors

1. **Printed p. 291 (PDF p. 292 — the unnumbered final page), Theme 5,
   probability exercises — two consecutive tasks are both numbered 6
   (confirmed by sight-reading the rendered page).** The page's tasks
   run 4, 5, **6, 6**: the first 6 asks which of four two-dice events
   is likely / certain / impossible; the second 6 asks for four
   two-dice outcomes to be ordered by likelihood. As printed the
   second task's number duplicates the first. **Teach-corrected
   wording:** renumber the second task **7**; both tasks' content is
   sound and no answer changes.
2. **Printed p. 141 — a false common-multiple statement (found at
   citation check, sight-verified on the rendered page).** The
   teaching text states "Бројот 35 е содржател на броевите 3 и 5"
   ("The number 35 is a multiple of the numbers 3 and 5") — but 35
   is **not** a multiple of 3 (3 × 11 = 33, 3 × 12 = 36).
   **Teach-corrected:** a common multiple of 3 and 5 is e.g. 30 (or
   read the line as "5 и 7", for which 35 is correct).
3. **Printed p. 145 — a false divisor in the summary strip (found at
   citation check, sight-verified).** The summary strip lists the
   divisors of 30 as "1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 10, 15 и 60" — **60 is not a
   divisor of 30**; the worked panel on the same page correctly ends
   "…15 и 30". **Teach-corrected:** the divisors of 30 are 1, 2, 3,
   5, 6, 10, 15, 30.
4. **Printed p. 234, task 5 — probable misprint (ruled at citation
   check).** A set of 6 art books is priced at 4,216.8 denars and one
   book bought singly at 702.8 denars, and the task asks how much
   less the 6 books cost as a set — but 6 × 702.8 = 4,216.8 exactly,
   so as printed the set saves **0 denars**. A set-buying task's
   whole point is a positive saving, so the two prices cannot both
   be the intended ones; no intended price can be reconstructed from
   the book, so no teach-corrected value exists. **Teach the task as
   printed** (answer as printed: no saving); the error is the
   price(s), not the method.
5. No other confirmed errors. All other worked examples, rule boxes
   and printed results that were recomputed during the read checked
   out, including: 47,586 + 28,342 = 75,928 (printed p. 125);
   234 · 3 = 702 by repeated addition and by decomposition, and
   237 · 5 = 1,185 (printed p. 164); 36 · 23 = 828, the grid value
   99 · 28 = 2,772 and the column form 32 · 24 = 768 (printed
   p. 167); 732 : 6 = 122 and 736 : 8 = 92 with their checks
   (printed p. 177); 28 : 5 = 5 remainder 3, recorded as 3/5
   (printed p. 174); the squares 1²–10² = 1, 4, 9, 16, 25, 36, 49,
   64, 81, 100 and the full 10×10 multiplication table (printed
   p. 156); the order-of-operations set 6 + 12 : 2 = 12,
   16 : 4 · 2 = 8, (24 − 9) + 4 · 3 = 27 (printed p. 182); the
   fraction-of-quantity values 1/6 of 18 = 3, 5/6 of 18 = 15 and
   4/9 of 252 cm = 112 cm (printed p. 196); the decimal comparisons
   2,1 < 2,8, 2,05 > 2,04, 3,42 < 3,46, 4,2 > 4,17 (printed p. 206);
   the decimal sums/differences 1,0 + 1,3 = 2,3, 1,2 + 0,8 = 2,
   2,4 − 2,0 = 0,4, 1,7 − 0,5 = 1,2 (printed pp. 212, 217); the
   ×10/100 shift values 0,36·10 = 3,6, 0,36·100 = 36, 3,18·10 = 31,8,
   3,18·100 = 318, and the ×10/100/1,000 table rows for 0,6 / 3,4 /
   7,2 / 0,56 / 1,25 (printed p. 231); the area-unit
   relations 1 dm² = 100 cm², 1 m² = 100 dm² = 10,000 cm²,
   1 dm² = 10,000 mm² (printed p. 260); and the bimodal mode example
   (modes 2 and 4, each occurring 3 times, printed p. 289).
   Because the read was contents + outcomes pages + heading scan +
   sampled interiors (see "How this book was read"), the "no other
   errors" statement covers the pages sight-read, not every exercise
   on all 291 pages.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting content: all 294 PDF pages render; the book is
  complete from cover (PDF p. 1) through the last numbered page
  (printed p. 290 = PDF p. 291) and the unnumbered final content
  page (PDF p. 292), blank page (PDF p. 293) and back cover
  (PDF p. 294). The CIP extent (290, [1] pp.) matches exactly, and
  the heading scan found every contents lesson at its stated page —
  no missing or duplicated pages.
- The duplicated task number 6 on printed p. 291 (Confirmed book
  errors № 1) is a defect of the printed book, faithfully held in
  this copy.
- The text layer is clean except for a handful of replacement
  characters (�) where display fonts are used (e.g. in a story
  passage at printed p. 201 and the example at printed p. 262);

ПЕТТО ОДДЕЛЕНИЕ + Grade 5 + page + 2022.*

### Citation check — Grade 5 (2026-11-07, checker ≠ reader)

22 anchors checked on exact PDF pages — **20 passed, 2 corrected, 0 not found**, verified from rendered pages. Corrections applied at assembly: (1) the percentages negative — percentage-of-a-quantity IS set as exercises inside the representation lesson (printed p. 65: 40% of 30 pupils; 25% of the child participants); the taught summary and the negative were both reworded (the "no separate lesson, no increase/discount" part stands); (2) the flagged p. 234 task-5 oddity is ruled a **probable misprint** (a set of 6 at exactly 6 × 702.8 = 4,216.8 denars saves 0 denars) and is filed as confirmed error 4, taught as printed. Two further confirmed errors were found by the check itself and added to the errors section: printed p. 141 ("35 is a multiple of the numbers 3 and 5" — false for 3) and printed p. 145 (60 listed among the divisors of 30). Special claims verified: the colophon (approval 26-1441/1 од 14.10.2022; ISBN 978-608-273-180-3; CIP 51(075.2)); the duplicated task 6 on the unnumbered final page (PDF p. 292); remainder-as-fraction on printed p. 174; the mode as the only average (printed p. 289); the time-zones lesson (printed p. 280). 21 placement pages spot-verified (158 lines); placement counts re-added exact.

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 6

> Book: *МАТЕМАТИКА 6* (Mathematics 6), Крутан Расими, Валентина Гоговска & Снежана Ристовска (Krutan Rasimi, Valentina Gogovska & Snežana Ristovska), Ministry of Education and Science of the Republic of North Macedonia, Skopje **2023**. Ministry-published textbook for Grade 6 (шесто одделение) of the nine-year primary school; approval decision 26-2185/1 of 15.09.2023 (below). Gogovska and Ristovska are also the Grade 7 book's authors (Band C, second section).
