### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page + 1** throughout the numbered body. Spot-verified at: PDF p. 3 = printed 2 (colophon); PDF p. 10 = printed 9; PDF p. 61 = printed 60; PDF p. 89 = printed 88; PDF p. 213 = printed 212; PDF p. 235 = printed 234; PDF p. 247 = printed 246; PDF p. 278 = printed 277; PDF p. 283 = printed 282; PDF p. 394 = printed 393 (checks made on rendered pages, not the text layer alone). PDF p. 1 is the cover and PDF p. 2 the unnumbered title page; the last content page is printed p. 393; PDF p. 395 carries folio 394 with a header only (blank body) and PDF p. 396 is the decorative back cover. The book prints no CIP extent, so completeness is verified against its own contents (below).

### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF has a **real text layer** (about 483,000 extracted characters over 396 pages), and the book was **read in full from that layer, page by page** (PDF pp. 1–396), including every lesson, worked example and self-assessment. The layer is glyph-scrambled in some display-type headings, in chart labels, and on the colophon page; those places were read from **rendered pages** instead (cover, title/colophon pages, contents, all error-check pages, and the final self-assessment pages, sight-read as renders). The complete contents (PDF pp. 5–8) were transcribed in full — the topic map below is the book's own contents with its printed page numbers. Worked examples across all five themes were recomputed; the errors below were each verified against a rendered page, not the extraction alone.

### Full topic/unit map (printed pages, from the book's own contents, PDF pp. 5–8)

Contents-page quirk (the book's own, not a copy defect): the contents spreads interleave the themes out of physical order — Тема 2's heading and first lessons are printed on the first contents spread before Тема 1's later lessons, and Тема 5 is listed before Тема 4's later lessons. Physically the book runs Тема 1 → 5 in order, as mapped here.

Units of area) — 337 · Плоштина на форми што можат да се поделат на правоаголници (Area of shapes that split into rectangles) — 340 · Плоштина на правоаголен триаголник (Area of a right triangle) — 345 · Проценување плоштина на неправилен многуаголник во квадратна мрежа (Estimating the area of an irregular polygon on a square grid) — 348 · Задачи за самооценување — 351

#### Тема 5 — РАБОТА СО ПОДАТОЦИ (Working with data), printed pp. 355–393
- Читање податоци од табели и дијаграми (Reading data from tables and diagrams) — 355 · Претставување податоци со табела на честота (Frequency tables) — 365 · Претставување податоци со линиски дијаграм (Line diagrams) — 367 · Претставување податоци со столбест дијаграм (Bar diagrams) — 371 · Истражување (Investigation) — 375 · Мода. Аритметичка средина (Mode. Arithmetic mean) — 379 · Ранг. Медијана (Range/rank. Median) — 382 · Сигурен настан, невозможен настан и еднакво веројатни настани (Certain event, impossible event, equally likely events) — 386 · Задачи за самооценување — 390 (checklist runs to printed p. 393)

### Everything taught (own summary)

- **Numbers (pp. 9–98).** Sets from scratch: a set as an undefined basic concept; writing sets by listing, by a describing property and in Venn diagrams; ∈/∉, empty set; cardinality n(A); equal and equinumerous sets; subset/proper subset; finite vs infinite sets — a full first set-theory strand. Then the number systems in widening rings: N and N₀ (predecessor/successor), naturals on the number line with point coordinates; **classes and place value to billions** (units/thousands/millions/billions classes; 1,283,504 read aloud as a worked example); comparing/ordering naturals; **rounding naturals to the nearest 10/100/1000 with printed digit rules**; natural-number sequences, including finding terms from a general term (e.g. 10n+25); **Roman numerals** with additive and subtractive rules (IL = 49, LIX = 59, CIV = 104; table 11–20); **integers Z** motivated by direction from 0 and temperatures above/below zero — Z⁺, Z⁻, 0 neither, N = Z⁺; integers on the number line; opposite numbers −a (with −(−9) = 9); comparing/ordering integers; integer sequences; fraction sequences (proper/improper/"apparent" fractions, counting by unit fractions on pictures and the line); **mixed number ↔ improper fraction** both ways (7/4 = 1¾; 103/4 = 25¾ by long division), and positive rational numbers defined; decimal place value (tenths/hundredths/thousandths; decimal fractions; 0.25 = ¼); decimal sequences; rounding decimals; expanding/reducing fractions, equal fractions, trailing zeros in decimals, irreducible fractions; mixed numbers on the number line; comparing fractions (same denominator, same numerator, common denominator, mixed vs improper) and comparing decimals (7/2 vs 1.4 by three methods); **percentages** — 1% = 1/100, fraction/decimal/% conversions (236% = 2.36), a 10% discount 800 → 720 denars; **ratio (размер)** — 4:1 dark:white chocolates, 2:15 teachers:pupils, splitting a class of 36 in ratio 4:5 into 16 girls and 20 boys; map scale appears in the theme self-assessment (1 cm = 5 km; 9 cm → 45 km).
- **Geometry (pp. 101–204).** Point, line, ray, segment and segment length; adding/subtracting segment lengths arithmetically and graphically; mutually parallel and mutually perpendicular lines, drawn with two rulers/set square; **distance from a point to a line** as the perpendicular foot (0 when the point lies on the line); **perpendicular bisector (симетрала) of a segment** and midpoint; the **angle** in full — vertex, arms, interior/exterior, four naming ways, and the type ladder zero 0°, acute, right 90°, straight 180°, obtuse, full 360°; adjacent, supplementary-adjacent (напоредни) and vertical (накрсни) angles; measuring and drawing angles with a protractor (worked 37° and 143°); complementary and supplementary angles; **angle bisector**; **circle and disc** — centre, radius, chord, diameter d = 2r, compass construction, k(O,r)/K(O,r) notation; positions of a point vs a circle, a line vs a circle (0/1/2 common points), and of two circles (separate, nested, touching internally/externally, intersecting; concentric circles named in an exercise); polygons — sides/vertices/interior/exterior angles, regular vs irregular, triangle classification by sides and by angles (legs and hypotenuse named); **diagonals** — n−3 from one vertex and the total n(n−3)/2 (worked: octagon 20, dodecagon 54); **altitude and orthocentre** (at the right-angle vertex in a right triangle, outside in an obtuse one); **median (тежишна линија) and centroid (тежиште)**; **circumcircle** (centre = intersection of the sides' perpendicular bisectors) and **incircle** (centre = intersection of the angle bisectors); the side–angle relation in a triangle and its converse; **angle sum 180°** and interior + exterior = 180° (worked isosceles: vertex 38° → base angles 71°, exterior 109°); 2D↔3D — polyhedra (faces ѕидови, edges, vertices; cube 8/12/6, cuboid, triangular prism 6/9/5) against round solids (cylinder = 2 circles + curved surface); nets of prisms and pyramids; net of a cylinder; the **rectangular coordinate system in all four quadrants**; axial symmetry w.r.t. the coordinate axes and w.r.t. lines parallel to the axes; **translation** (original/image, coordinate changes); **rotation** (centre, angle, direction; 90° about the origin or about a vertex).
- **Operations with numbers (pp. 209–294).** Mental-first arithmetic: adding/subtracting when one number is near the nearest 10/100/1000 (compensation, and how a sum/difference changes when an addend, minuend or subtrahend changes — but see error 3); written addition to 1,000,000 (expanded, horizontal, column; 356,879 + 435,442 = 792,321, verified) with the commutative and associative properties; subtraction to 1,000,000 with borrowing worked in detail; m + 0, m − 0; subtraction as the inverse of addition, with unknown addend/minuend/subtrahend in box equations; × by full tens/hundreds/thousands by place shift (456×10/100/1000; 25×30 = 750); the properties of × in N₀ (commutative, associative, distributive over + and −); × when one factor is near a full ten/hundred (19×7, 299×6 = 1,794, 298×8 = 2,384 shown four ways); doubling/halving the factors, including the **ancient-Egyptian doubling method** (14×37 = 518) — a distinctive historical method taught as content; 2/3/4-digit × 1-digit (242×4 = 968 by several strategies); × by two-digit numbers (13×25 four ways; 247×28 = 6,916; 3,561×62); division as finding an unknown factor; two-digit ÷ one-digit with the **remainder written as a fraction** (22:3 = 7⅓; 31:9 = 3⁴⁄₉; distributive property of division over +/−); three-digit ÷ one-digit (long division 497:7 = 71; 124:5 = 24 r 4 → 25 plates needed); division to 1,000,000 by a two-digit number (312:24 = 13); numerical expressions and order of operations; **equations solved by the box-substitution method** (140:(x−3) = 10 → x = 17; 15x + 32 = 92) and applied to word problems; **divisors and multiples** as sets (D₁₂, S₁₂); factorising a two-digit number into factor pairs; prime and composite numbers (1 is neither); **prime factorisation** (165 = 3·5·11; ladder for 72; factor tree for 48; Goldbach's statement as an intro activity); **divisibility tests** for 2, 5, 10; for 9, 3, 6 (with a digit-sum justification on 2,745); for 4 and 8; common divisors and **GCD (НЗД)**; common multiples and **LCM (НЗС(36,48) = 144 by prime factors)**; adding/subtracting proper fractions with equal denominators (6/16 + 9/16 = 15/16; the **"spider" (пајак) web diagram of equal fractions**, 2/3 + 10/15 = 4/3); adding/subtracting improper fractions and mixed numbers with equal denominators; decimal pairs summing to 1 (two decimal places) and to 10 (one decimal place); adding and subtracting decimals with equal places (16.45 + 3.53 = 19.98, verified); +/− decimals where one is near a whole number (13.9 + 6.4 = 20.3); +/− decimals with different numbers of places, padded with zeros (465.38 − 354.60 = 110.78, verified); × a decimal by a natural number (place-value expansion; 1.7×3 = 5.1; 5.45×5); × a decimal by a one-decimal number via rectangle area; dividing a decimal (1–2 decimal places) by a one-digit number (62.5:5 = 12.5).
- **Measurement (pp. 297–351).** The full metric ladders, **including the decametre/hectometre and hectogram/decagram steps most books skip**: length km–mm (167 dam = 1,670 m; 245 hm = 24,500 m; 250 dm = 25 m); mass t–mg (1 dag = 1/100 kg, 1 hg = 1/10 kg; 324 kg = 0.324 t; 324 kg = 3,240,000 dg); capacity kℓ–mℓ (67,000 mℓ = 67 ℓ; a barrel total 67 + 45.5 + 50 + 15 + 25 = 202.5 ℓ, verified); **едноимени (single-name) measured numbers** and converting them; **повеќеимени (multi-name) numbers** (5,896 g = 5 kg 8 hg 9 dag 6 g; 9 km 6 hm 8 m = 9,608 m; 8 daℓ 8 ℓ 7 dℓ = 88.7 ℓ); comparing measurements (12.34 ℓ vs 1.258 daℓ = 12.58 ℓ); estimating length/mass/capacity, including deliberately wrong estimates for pupils to judge; **perimeter of a polygon**; time measures (12 h 10 min + 55 min = 13 h 5 min; parts of an hour as fractions); digital and analogue clocks, 12-hour vs 24-hour, AM/PM; comparing time (a film marathon of 26.5 h); time intervals (bus departures every 57 min, worked); calendar work; **money** — denars, euro and dollar cents, an exchange-rate list (1 € ≈ 61.5 denars; a 3 € sandwich = 184.5 → 185 denars; 235 euros 20 cents = 235.20 €); **area units** km²–mm² plus are/hectare/dekar (1 a = 100 m²; 1 ha = 10,000 m²) with conversions by 100s (13 cm² = 0.13 dm²; 256,871 mm² = 25 dm² 68 cm² 71 mm², verified); area of shapes that split into rectangles (two different splittings both give 14 cm²); **area of a right triangle P = a·b/2**, including finding an unknown leg (P = 41.6 cm², a = 8 → b = 10.4 cm, verified); estimating the area of an irregular polygon on a square grid, closing with a map-of-the-country grid task (fraction of the country mountainous / under lakes).
- **Working with data (pp. 355–393).** Reading data from tables and line/bar/pie diagrams, with **frequency (честота/фреквенција)** defined on shoe-size sales data; **frequency tables** (a 30-pupil test-grade table, recomputed and exact); **line diagrams**, including extending the line beyond the data to read further values (a dripping tap reaching 80 ℓ at 4 h) and currency-conversion line graphs as exercises; **bar diagrams** (equal-width bars, choosing the scale; matching a pie diagram to the bar diagram that shows the same data); a full **investigation (истражување)** strand — question, plan, a printed model questionnaire, tally marks, organising into tables and diagrams, analysis, answering and posing new questions; **mode and arithmetic mean** (grade averages per pupil, recomputed and exact); **rank list, range and median**, including the even-count median (Petar's spending: mean 280 denars, median from the middle pair, both verified); and a first probability strand in words only — elementary events, **certain, impossible, random and equally likely events** on spinners, dice and ball bags. Final self-assessment items were recomputed (oil-consumption median/mean task; newborn-mass task; the "Задача за 5+" score-frequency table, frequencies summing to 60, verified; lily percentage 160/320 = 50%; mean games (8·3+0)/4 = 6; range of 2,3,4,5,6 = 4).

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 6 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (PDF pp. 5–8) has no entry for any of the following, and full-text searches of the extracted layer return zero hits where noted.

- **No Pythagoras theorem** (search: Питагора, 0 hits) and no square roots or powers — "степен" occurs only as degrees (angles, Celsius).
- **No circle measurement** — no circumference, no area of a circle (searches: обиколка на круг / плоштина на круг, 0 hits); the circle is construction and position work only.
- **No volume or surface area of solids** (search: волумен, 0 hits) — 3D work stops at faces/edges/vertices and nets; зафатнина occurs only as capacity of liquids.
- **No multiplication or division of fractions** — fraction operations are +/− with **equal denominators only**; there is no adding/subtracting with unlike denominators either (the "spider" diagram rewrites one fraction to an equal one with the other's denominator, but no general unlike-denominator lesson exists).
- **No arithmetic with negative integers** — integers are introduced, placed, opposed and compared, and appear in sequences, but there is no lesson adding, subtracting, multiplying or dividing them.
- **No similarity or congruence** (searches: сличност / подударност, 0 hits), no bearings, no constructions with compass beyond the circle and the two bisectors.
- **No proportion as an equation** (search: пропорција, 0 hits) — ratio is taught, and direct-proportion situations appear inside exercises, but there is no proportion/процентуална сметка lesson.
- **No probability values** — probability is the qualitative certain/impossible/equally-likely language only; no probability as a fraction or number, no expected outcomes.
- **No data beyond mean/mode/median/range** — no quartiles, no grouped data, no scatter work.

### Confirmed book errors

Each error below was recomputed by the reader and verified against the rendered printed page (not the text extraction alone). Teach-corrected wording is given for each.

1. **Printed p. 60, Задачи за вежби 1а (integer sequences).** The printed sequence runs −11, −8, −5, −2, **0**, 3, 6 — it breaks its own +3 rule at the fifth term. Correct continuation from −2 is **1, 4, 7** (the printed 0 should be 1, and the following printed terms are shifted accordingly). Teach: "Continue by adding 3 each time: −11, −8, −5, −2, 1, 4, 7."
2. **Printed p. 88, Example 1б (comparing decimals).** The working prints "1324/100 < 1354/100, бидејќи 1324 < **1353**" — the second numerator in the justification is misprinted 1353 for 1354. The conclusion 13,24 < 13,54 is correct. Teach the comparison with 1354 on both sides.
3. **Printed p. 212, rule box (how a difference changes).** The printed rule says (in Macedonian) that if the subtrahend in a difference is increased by some number greater than zero, "тогаш и разликата ќе се зголеми за истиот тој број" — the difference will also **increase** by that number. That is backwards: increasing the subtrahend **decreases** the difference by the same amount — and the book's own Example 6 on the same page (2456 − 389, subtrahend rounded up by 11, 11 added back) follows the correct behaviour. Teach-corrected: "Ако намалителот се зголеми за некој број, разликата ќе се **намали** за истиот тој број."
4. **Printed p. 234, Воведна активност (introductory activity).** "Производот на три последователни броеви е 730. Кои се тие броеви?" — **no three consecutive integers have product 730** (8·9·10 = 720; 9·10·11 = 990; 730 = 2·5·73). The intended product is evidently 720, giving 8, 9, 10. Teach with 720.
5. **Printed p. 234, Example 1.** The text says Arben wrote "множителот **232**" in expanded form, but the factor being multiplied is 242 and the expansion (200 + 40 + 2)·4 = 968 printed below is correct for 242. "232" is a misprint; teach with 242.
6. **Printed p. 246, Example 3.** The statement reads "Разликата на броевите **894697** и 418452 намали ја 45 пати", but the worked line computes (894687 − 418452) : 45 = 476235 : 45 = 10583. With the printed 894697 the difference is 476245, and 476245 : 45 = 10583 remainder 10 — not exact. Teach-corrected: read the minuend as **894687** throughout; the printed answer 10583 then stands.
7. **Printed p. 277, Example 2 (subtracting mixed numbers).** 9⁴⁄₁₁ − 4⁷⁄₁₁ is worked as = 4 + (15/11 − **11/11**) = 4⁴⁄₁₁. After borrowing one whole, the fraction step must be 15/11 − 7/11 = 8/11. The correct result is **4⁸⁄₁₁** (check: 103/11 − 51/11 = 52/11 = 4⁸⁄₁₁). Teach 4⁸⁄₁₁.
8. **Printed p. 282, Example 1б (subtracting decimals).** The worked solution of 16,59 − 3,35 is corrupted throughout: 16,59 is converted to 16⁴⁵⁄₁₀₀ (= 1645/100; it should be 59/100), the subtrahend 3,35 to 353/100 (should be 335/100), the printed subtraction 1645 − 353 is given as 1314 (it is 1292), and the column version below silently changes the subtrahend to 3,45 (16,59 − 3,45 = 13,14). The printed final answer **13,14 is wrong**; correctly 1659/100 − 335/100 = 1324/100 = **13,24**. Teach 13,24.
9. **Printed p. 351, Self-assessment task 2 (measurement).** The field's area is printed as "400 **cm2**" while its leg is 20 **m** and hypotenuse ≈ 45 m. In cm² the data are incoherent; read as **400 m²** everything coheres exactly (other leg = 2·400/20 = 40 m; perimeter ≈ 105 m; wire for four rounds ≈ 420 m). Teach with 400 m².
10. **Printed p. 380, Example 2 (arithmetic mean) — pupil-name inconsistency.** The introduction names the four pupils "Александар, Петар, Маја и Сена", but the grades table's first row is **Борјан**, and the worked text then computes Borjan's row (4 + 3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 4 + 5 + 4 = 32; 32 : 8 = 4 — arithmetic verified) while calling the result "средниот успех на Александар". One name must be chosen; the table's Борјан is the consistent reading.
11. **Printed p. 88, Example 1а (writing a decimal as a mixed number) — found at citation check, sight-verified.** The working prints "3,4 = 13⁴⁄₁₀ = 34/10" — the leading "1" in 13⁴⁄₁₀ does not belong; **3⁴⁄₁₀** is meant. Same kind of slip as Error 2 on the same page. Teach 3,4 = 3⁴⁄₁₀ = 34/10.

Production slips (not mathematical errors, noted only): printed p. 77, the heading "Пример 2" is duplicated ("Пример 2. Пример 2."); printed p. 339, the area table prints "1 cm2 = 1/10000 m = 0,0001 m2" with the square missing on the middle term (value correct); printed p. 346, Example 2 ends "другата катета на правоаголникот" ("the other leg of the rectangle") where триаголникот (triangle) is meant (value 10.4 cm correct).

Checked and explicitly **not** errors: printed p. 77 task 3д is the mixed number 1¾ = 42/x → x = 24 (an extraction misread suggested 13/4); printed p. 213 task *9 items are deliberately right/wrong methods for pupils to judge; printed p. 280's addition 16.45 + 3.53 = 19.98 is correct. The book has **no printed answers section**, so no answer key could be checked.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting content: all 396 PDF pages are present and in order; every contents entry was found at its printed page; the numbered body runs printed pp. 2–394 (p. 394 a header-only blank page) between the cover/title pages and the decorative back cover. The book prints no CIP extent to check against (no CIP in this edition — see Book facts).
- The text layer is glyph-scrambled in places (display headings, chart labels, the colophon) — an extraction defect of the file, not missing content; all such passages needed for this filing were read from renders.
- Contents spreads list the themes interleaved out of physical order (noted in the map) — the book's own layout, faithfully present in the copy.

*Citations for this book: North Macedonia + Математика 6 + Grade 6 + page + 2023.*

### Citation check — Grade 6 (2026-11-07, checker ≠ reader)

22 anchors checked on exact PDF pages — **22 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found**; 12 placement lines spot-verified, no page wrong. Special claims verified: the book prints **no ISBN, CIP or COBISS anywhere** (whole-PDF greps return 0 for ISBN/CIP/COBISS/Каталогизација; colophon render and decorative back cover sight-read clean); the approval бр. 26-2185/1 од 15.09.2023 verbatim on the colophon render; the printed p. 351 "400 cm²" unit error (sight-read; the 400 m² coherence recomputed); the printed p. 380 pupil-name inconsistency (sight-read at 300 dpi; all four grade rows recompute exactly); all ten filed errors verbatim and recomputed. One addition from the check: printed p. 88, Example 1а prints "3,4 = 13⁴⁄₁₀" (a spurious leading 1) — filed as error 11 at assembly.

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 7

> **Математика 7** (Mathematics 7) — Валентина Гоговска, Снежана Ристовска (Valentina Gogovska, Snezhana Ristovska) — АЛБИ ДОО (ALBI DOO), Скопје (Skopje), 2025 — held copy `pdfs/g07.pdf`, 386 PDF pages, 25,520,992 bytes, SHA-256 `bc5b120d8fc8a1ea7f5ba2550fac42db50d14bfe3f8c554b367b333788aa078c`.

**Book facts exactly as printed.** The book is in Macedonian (Cyrillic), including all front matter. The cover (PDF p. 1, image-only, sight-read as a rendered image) prints "МАТЕМАТИКА" (Mathematics), "ЗА СЕДМО ОДДЕЛЕНИЕ ВО ОСНОВНОТО ОБРАЗОВАНИЕ" (For seventh grade in primary education), a large "7", the word "УЧЕБНИК" (Textbook) and the publisher's АЛБИ logo; the chalkboard artwork itself carries mathematics (P = 6a², a three-set Venn diagram, 3x + 4 = 6x + 1) — artwork, not text. The title page (PDF p. 3) prints the two authors, "Д-р Валентина Гоговска" (Dr Valentina Gogovska) and "Снежана Ристовска" (Snezhana Ristovska) — the same Gogovska + Ristovska pairing as the Grade 6 book, without Rasimi — then "МАТЕМАТИКА 7" and "АЛБИ". The colophon (PDF p. 4, sight-read as a rendered image) prints: © 2025 АЛБИ ДОО, Скопје; publisher (издавач) АЛБИ ДОО, Скопје; editor (уредник) Елена Стефановска (Elena Stefanovska); lector (лектор) Ивана Кузмановска (Ivana Kuzmanovska); design credited to Паблик дипломаси, Скопје (as rendered); printer (печатница) ПОЛИЕСТЕРДЕЈ ДООЕЛ, Скопје; print run (тираж) 14500; and the ministry approval "број 26-913/1 од 22.08.2025" (decision no. 26-913/1 of 22.08.2025, Ministry of Education and Science). The CIP block on the same page reads: 373.3.016:51(075.2) — ГОГОВСКА, Валентина, *Математика 7* — Скопје: АЛБИ, 2025. – 384 стр. (384 pages): илустр. (illustrated); 195 × 265 мм; **ISBN 978-608-241-054-8**; **COBISS.MK-ID 66752517**. PDF p. 5 is the authors' unnumbered introduction (ВОВЕД, Introduction); PDF pp. 7–8 print the contents (Содржина). The held file is the reduced-size copy (source filename "Matematika 7 _Mak_namalen.pdf"); its PDF metadata show an encrypted PDF 1.6 (print: no, copy: yes, change: no, AES) produced by Acrobat Distiller 11.0, with 2026 distiller dates in the metadata — an artefact of the reduction, not the book's date; the book's year is 2025 per the colophon and CIP.

**Page offset — verified.** PDF page = printed page + 2. Spot-verified at six points against the printed folios sight-read on renders: PDF 11 = printed 9 (the first lesson), PDF 100 = printed 98, PDF 250 = printed 248, PDF 384 = printed 382, PDF 385 = printed 383, and PDF 386 = printed 384 (blank, see Held-copy defects). All printed pages cited below are printed pages.

**How this book was read.** The held copy has **no embedded text layer at all** (image-only pages), so the whole book was read from an OCR text layer produced for this read: every one of the 386 pages was rendered at 150 dpi and OCR'd with tesseract (Macedonian model), giving 568,794 OCR characters with page markers, and that layer was read page by page for all content pages (printed pp. 9–383). The cover, title page, colophon, introduction and contents were sight-read as rendered images, as were ten further pages carrying flagged computations (printed pp. 47, 53, 56, 113, 115, 171, 231, 257, 282, 295 — see Confirmed errors). OCR of mathematics is noisy (digits and symbols are regularly confused), so no worked example was treated as an error on OCR evidence alone: every suspect was re-checked against the rendered page before being filed. Completeness was checked mechanically: a pixel-variance scan over all 386 page renders found exactly three uniformly blank pages (PDF pp. 2, 6 and 386), all of them the book's own blank leaves. Diagram-only content (constructions, graphs, pie charts) was read from the OCR text plus the contents; individual diagram details that OCR cannot carry were not separately sight-read except where flagged above.

**Full topic/unit map** (printed pages, from the book's own contents, PDF pp. 7–8):

*Тема 1: Броеви и операции со броеви (Theme 1: Numbers and operations with numbers)* — Операции со множества (Operations with sets) 9; Својства на пресек и унија на множества (Properties of intersection and union of sets) 16; Подредени парови (Ordered pairs) 21; Споредување цели броеви (Comparing integers) 26; Апсолутна вредност на цел број (Absolute value of an integer) 31; Собирање цели броеви (Adding integers) 36; Својства на собирањето цели броеви (Properties of adding integers) 42; Равенки од видот x + a = b и одземање цели броеви (Equations of the form x + a = b and subtracting integers) 46; Множење цели броеви (Multiplying integers) 50; Својства на множењето цели броеви (Properties of multiplying integers) 55; Делење цели броеви (Dividing integers) 59; Вредност на бројниот израз (Value of a numerical expression) 63; Степен со природен изложител (Power with a natural exponent) 68; Квадрати и квадратни корени до 100 (Squares and square roots to 100) 73; Споредување дропки (Comparing fractions) 77; Периодични децимални броеви (Periodic decimal numbers) 82; Дел од цело со дропки и проценти (Part of a whole with fractions and percents) 86; Собирање дропки (Adding fractions) 90; Одземање дропки (Subtracting fractions) 94; Множење дропки (Multiplying fractions) 98; Делење дропки (Dividing fractions) 103; Собирање и одземање децимални броеви со различен број децимални места (Adding and subtracting decimals with different numbers of decimal places) 108; Множење и делење на децимални броеви (Multiplying and dividing decimal numbers) 112; Размер и дел од цело (Ratio and part of a whole) 117; Правопропорционалност во реални ситуации (Direct proportionality in real situations) 121.
*Тема 2: Геометрија (Theme 2: Geometry)* — Кружница и круг (Circle and disc) 125; Тангента (Tangent) 133; Графичко собирање и одземање на отсечки (Graphical addition and subtraction of segments) 136; Конструирање паралелни и нормални прави и симетрала на отсечка (Constructing parallel and perpendicular lines and the bisector of a segment) 142; Конструкции со образовен софтвер (Constructions with educational software) 148; Основни поими за агли и полурамнини (Basic concepts of angles and half-planes) 152; Мерење, проценување и цртање агли (Measuring, estimating and drawing angles) 157; Централен агол (Central angle) 162; Операции со агли (Operations with angles) 168; Графичко собирање и одземање на агли (Graphical addition and subtraction of angles) 173; Конструирање симетрала на агол и агли 60°, 30°, 15°, 90°, 45° (Constructing the angle bisector and the angles 60°, 30°, 15°, 90°, 45°) 177; Трансверзала и паралелни прави (Transversal and parallel lines) 182; Конвексни и неконвексни многуаголници (Convex and non-convex polygons) 186; Основни поими за триаголник (Basic concepts of the triangle) 190; Конструирање триаголници (Constructing triangles) 194; Конструирање опишана и впишана кружница на триаголник (Constructing the circumscribed and inscribed circle of a triangle) 199; Четириаголници според паралелноста на страните (Quadrilaterals by the parallelism of their sides) 203; Збир на агли во четириаголник (Angle sum in a quadrilateral) 208; Паралелограм и негови својства (Parallelogram and its properties) 211; Видови паралелограми (Kinds of parallelograms) 217; Осна симетрија во координатен систем (Axial symmetry in a coordinate system) 223; Транслација во координатен систем (Translation in a coordinate system) 228; Ротација на многуаголници (Rotation of polygons) 233; Претставување на 3Д-фигури во проекции (Representing 3D shapes in projections) 237.
*Тема 3: Алгебра (Theme 3: Algebra)* — Основни поими: членови, изрази, равенки (Basic concepts: terms, expressions, equations) 241; Упростување на линеарен израз, вредност на израз (Simplifying a linear expression, value of an expression) 247; Формули, изразување на една променлива преку друга (Formulae, expressing one variable through another) 251; Линеарна равенка со една непозната (Linear equation in one unknown) 255; Низи од цели броеви (Sequences of integers) 259; Претставување на едноставни функции (Representing simple functions) 264; График на линеарна функција и координатни парови (Graph of a linear function and coordinate pairs) 268; Графици паралелни со оската x или оската y (Graphs parallel to the x-axis or the y-axis) 273.
*Тема 4: Мерење (Theme 4: Measurement)* — Мерење должина: инструменти и единици (Measuring length: instruments and units) 277; Мерење маса: инструменти и претворање единици (Measuring mass: instruments and converting units) 285; Мерење зафатнина и единици (Measuring volume/capacity and units) 289; Време: 12-часовен и 24-часовен систем (Time: 12-hour and 24-hour systems) 294; Пресметување временски интервали и работа со графици (Calculating time intervals and working with graphs) 302; Периметар на триаголник и четириаголник (Perimeter of triangle and quadrilateral) 310; Плоштина на составени 2Д-фигури (Area of composite 2D shapes) 317; Зафатнина и нејзини единици (Volume and its units) 321; Зафатнина на квадар и коцка (Volume of cuboid and cube) 326; Плоштина на коцка и квадар од нивните мрежи (Surface area of cube and cuboid from their nets) 330.
*Тема 5: Работа со податоци (Theme 5: Working with data)* — Планирање истражување (Planning an investigation) 335; Собирање и организирање податоци (Collecting and organising data) 342; Столбести и кружни дијаграми (Bar and pie diagrams) 347; Цртање и толкување линиски дијаграми и пиктограми (Drawing and interpreting line diagrams and pictograms) 353; Модална класа и дијаграм на фреквенција за групирани податоци (Modal class and frequency diagram for grouped data) 360; Проценување и пресметување на аритметичка средина, споредување на две множества податоци (Estimating and calculating the arithmetic mean, comparing two data sets) 366; Исходи. Веројатност (Outcomes. Probability) 373; Експериментална и теоретска веројатност (Experimental and theoretical probability) 378.

**Everything taught** (own summary; printed pages are lesson starts).

*Theme 1 — Numbers and operations with numbers (pp. 9–124).* Set operations come first: intersection (пресек), union (унија) and difference (разлика) worked through Venn diagrams and word contexts (pupils in sports and art sections, discounts joined by "and"/"or"), then the properties of ∩ and ∪ (commutative and associative) and counting members by inclusion — from 20 pupils in the mathematics section and 8 in both, 12 do mathematics only (p. 16). Ordered pairs introduce the Cartesian product A × B drawn as a Cartesian scheme (Декартова шема), with A² notation and the point that a pair is not a set (p. 21). Integers: the set Z containing N, ordering on the number line, ≥ and ≤ met in words ("at least −3 °C" becomes T ≥ −3) (p. 26); absolute value |a| as distance from zero, with opposite numbers and true/false statement judging that includes deliberately false statements for pupils to reject (p. 31). Adding integers runs through the number line and the absolute-value rule for same and different signs (p. 36), then the properties of addition, including a bus problem 17 + 10 − 3 = 24 passengers (p. 42). Subtraction of integers is *defined* as adding the opposite number, inside the lesson that also solves x + a = b by adding the opposite to both sides (p. 46). Multiplication is modelled by a point moving on the number line and reduced to the sign rules with |a| · |b| (p. 50); its properties include distribution over addition and subtraction, checked both ways to −1666 (p. 55). Division notes that a quotient need not exist in Z and that division by zero is meaningless (p. 59). Numerical expressions fix the ranks of operations (powers third, × and ÷ second, + and − first), brackets, the rules for removing brackets, and expressions containing | | (p. 63). Powers with a natural exponent follow, with a⁰ = 1 stated as an agreement, a bacteria-doubling context reaching 2¹² = 4096, and Marko's flowers 3, 6, 12, 24, 48, 96 summing to 189 (p. 68). Squares and square roots to 100 come with the full list of squares and the side of a square from its area, noting ± before restricting to positive roots (p. 73). Fractions: comparing by common denominator, common numerator, diagrams and decimals, with proper < 1 ≤ improper (p. 77); periodic decimals classified as finite (конечен), purely periodic (чисто периодичен) and mixed periodic (мешано периодичен), derived by long division (1 : 9 = 0.111…, 7 : 12 = 0.5833…), with 0.999… = 1 posed as a thinking question (p. 82); parts of a whole in fractions and percents — 12% of 1000 lights is 120 — with discounts and price rises (p. 86). Adding fractions with unlike denominators uses the LCM, mixed numbers are handled two ways, and a distinctive benchmark estimation rounds fractions to 0, ½ or 1 before calculating (p. 90); subtraction likewise (p. 94). Multiplication covers natural × fraction, fraction of a quantity and fraction × fraction through area diagrams (p. 98); division uses the reciprocal, and a fraction divided by a natural number is done two ways — divide the numerator or multiply the denominator (p. 103). Decimals with different numbers of places are added and subtracted by padding zeros (Ana's shopping: 82.50 + 135 + 97.75 = 315.25, change from 500 is 184.75) (p. 108); multiplication and division use the count-the-places rule (6.4 : 10.24 = 0.625), and quotients are rounded to fit the story — 505.6 becomes 505 packages, while buses are rounded up (p. 112, contains confirmed error E1). Ratio is tied to part of a whole (30 cubes split 2 : 3 gives 12 and 18; part ratios 2 : 5 and 3 : 5), with map-scale exercises at 1 : 200 000 (p. 117). The theme ends with direct proportionality (правопропорционалност) solved by the unitary method — Sofia reads 60 pages in 2 hours, so 150 in 5 — plus currency exchange, recipes and fuel use (p. 121).

*Theme 2 — Geometry (pp. 125–238).* The circle lesson revises G6 and adds the arc (кружен лак) with three-letter naming, then positions of two circles decided by comparing centre distance with the sums and differences of the radii, and drawing to scale (p. 125). The tangent is perpendicular to the radius at the point of contact; secant is defined, and two tangents are drawn from an external point (p. 133). Segment lengths are added and subtracted graphically with compasses, and collinearity is tested by AC = AB + BC (p. 136); then come the compass constructions of parallel and perpendicular lines and of the perpendicular bisector (p. 142), repeated in educational software (GeoGebra is named in a task) (p. 148). Angles restart from half-planes (полурамнини) and the convex/non-convex (reflex) distinction by the segment test (p. 152); measuring, estimating and drawing include reflex angles read as 360° − 150° = 210° (p. 157). The central angle owns its arc, and equal central angles give equal chords and equal arcs (p. 162). Operations with angles are done in degrees, minutes and seconds — 45.5° = 45°30′, sums checked against 360°, subtraction with borrowing (103°17′34″ − 48°46′38″ = 54°30′56″) (p. 168) — then graphically, by copying, adding and subtracting with compasses (p. 173), and by constructing the bisector and the angles 60°, 30°, 15°, 90°, 45°, 120°, 135° and 37°30′ (p. 177). A transversal cutting parallel lines gives interior/exterior, corresponding (согласни), alternate (наизменични) and same-side interior angles, with the parallel-line equalities and the supplementary same-side pair (p. 182). Polygons are sorted convex/non-convex by the diagonal test (p. 186). The triangle lesson adds the midline (средна линија): it joins the midpoints, is parallel to the third side and half its length, and cuts the triangle into four congruent triangles (sides 10, 12, 14 give midlines 5, 6, 7) (p. 190). Triangle constructions SSS, SAS, ASA (p. 194) are followed by constructing the circumcircle and incircle (p. 199). Quadrilaterals are classified by parallelism — parallelogram (two pairs), трапез trapezium (one pair), трапезоид (none), делтоид kite, and the isosceles trapezium (p. 203); interior angles sum to 360° and exterior angles to 360°, the latter derived (p. 208). The parallelogram's properties (opposite sides and angles equal, consecutive angles supplementary, diagonals bisecting each other, a diagonal making two congruent triangles, in the book's words "иста форма и големина", the same shape and size) come with converses in the tasks (p. 211); rectangle (equal diagonals, a circumcircle exists), rhombus (perpendicular diagonals that bisect the angles, an incircle exists) and square follow, with checking a frame is rectangular by measuring its diagonals (p. 217). Transformations are done in coordinates: axial symmetry across the axes and lines parallel to them (p. 223), translation by adding to the coordinates (p. 228, contains confirmed error E2), and rotation of polygons — 90° clockwise and anticlockwise about a vertex or a point, 60° about the origin read from the grid (p. 233). The theme closes with 3D shapes in projections: front (фронтална), top/plan (планска) and side (профилна) views of prism, cylinder, pyramid, cone and machine parts (p. 237).

*Theme 3 — Algebra (pp. 241–274).* The basics lesson names terms (членови), coefficient (коефициент), the main value, like terms (слични членови) and the opposite term, and builds expressions from shopping and geometry contexts (p. 241). Simplifying a linear expression collects like terms and expands brackets — 4x + 3x + 2x + 5 = 9x + 5, worth 95 at x = 10; −2(3x + 5) − 4x + 3 = −10x − 7, worth 23 at x = −3 (p. 247). Formulae are rearranged to express one variable through the others, e.g. the taxi fare C = 80 + 50k solved for k (p. 251). The linear equation in one unknown is solved by inverse operations, including equations with brackets; Martina's example (250 denars, packs at 40, keep 30) gives x = 5.5 and the book reads it back into the story — she buys 5 packs and keeps 50 (p. 255). Sequences of integers use term notation a₁, a₂, … and the general term (општ член): 5, 8, 11, 14, 17 has seventh term 23; a savings sequence yields 30n + 20 (p. 259). Functions are introduced as dependence and represented four ways — words, table, formula, graph — with f(x) notation and a genuinely piecewise example, a shop discount f(P) = P, 0.95P or 0.90P by spending band (p. 264). The linear function y = ax + b (a ≠ 0) is graphed from coordinate pairs, and points are tested against the graph — (1, 5) does not lie on y = 2x − 3 (p. 268). Graphs parallel to the axes close the theme: y = c horizontal, x = c vertical, with a constant-temperature example at 22 °C (p. 273).

*Theme 4 — Measurement (pp. 277–332).* Length instruments are compared analogue against digital (ruler, caliper, micrometer), and the theme's distinctive item is the micron (микрон, µm): 50 µm = 0.05 mm, a hair is about 70 µm, and plate layers of 1200 µm = 1.2 mm are stacked in a 2 cm box — 20 : 1.2 = 16.(6), so 16 layers (pp. 277–284). Mass instruments and conversions follow (p. 285), then measuring capacity (зафатнина) with the мензура (measuring cylinder) and the litre ladder including kℓ and hℓ, and single-name and multi-name numbers revisited (p. 289). Time runs from seconds to centuries, sets the 12-hour against the 24-hour system, and computes durations (the Jovanovikj family's zoo 10:30–12:00, museum 12:30–14:00, lunch 14:30–15:30, then 2 h 45 more to 18:15; a bus 11:30 → 14:15 takes 2 h 45) (pp. 294–301); intervals continue with timetables (Skopje–Veles trains), distance–time graphs with pauses (Igor's lunch 10:15–12:30 inside a 5 h 45 trip) and a Belgrade–New York time-zone exercise (p. 302). Perimeter of triangles and quadrilaterals includes expressing perimeter through a variable and inverse problems — a parallelogram side from its perimeter, a trapezium leg from its perimeter (p. 310). Area is extended to composite 2D shapes, split into squares, rectangles and right triangles or found by subtracting cut-outs (p. 317). Volume starts from the unit cube with the ×1000 unit ladder and 1 ℓ = 1 dm³ (p. 321), gives V = a·b·c for the cuboid and V = a³ for the cube (edge 6 → 216 cm³; a 10 × 5 × 8 m pool holds 400 m³) (p. 326), and ends with surface area from nets, P = 2(ab + ac + bc): 4 × 3 × 2 gives 52 cm², a 6 × 4 × 3 shed 108 m² (p. 330).

*Theme 5 — Working with data (pp. 335–383).* An investigation is planned end to end — problem, plan, collect by survey/interview/observation, organise, analyse, conclude — with cross-curricular topics (p. 335). Collecting and organising names discrete data (дискретни податоци), tallying, and grouping into equal-length intervals; the books-read data of 30 pupils fall 4 / 8 / 10 / 6 / 2 across the five intervals, and every printed count was recomputed from the listed values and matches (p. 342). Bar diagrams and pie (кружен) diagrams compute the sector angle per unit: 36 pupils give 10° each, 17 girls 170° and 19 boys 190°; 720 flowers give 0.5° each (pp. 347–352). Line diagrams (including negative temperatures in quadrants I and IV) and pictograms with keys follow (p. 353). Grouped data brings the modal class (модална класа), the class containing the median, and the frequency diagram (дијаграм на фреквенција) (p. 360). The mean is first estimated from a diagram, then calculated, alongside median, mode and range (ранг), and two data sets are compared — two cities' temperatures estimated at about 21 °C and 18 °C, with stability judged by the range (pp. 366–372). Probability opens with outcomes (исходи) and elementary events, mutually exclusive outcomes, and certain and impossible events (Mile's drawer of 10 socks: 6 black, 2 blue, 2 green) (p. 373); probability with equally likely outcomes is computed as a fraction, decimal and percent — a spinner lands on red with 4 favourable of 10 fields, on green with 0 — and a forecast's 0.7 rain probability is read as a decision about an umbrella (pp. 375–377). The last lesson separates experimental from theoretical probability: 7 successes in 10 trials give 0.7 against a theoretical 0.6; Lena's 50 coin tosses give 22 "писмо" (tails) — 0.44 against 0.5 — and the book states that more trials bring the experimental value closer to the theoretical; a coffee machine's last 100 choices (40/30/30) are compared with the equal ⅓ shares (pp. 378–383).

**Honest negatives** (basis: the contents above, read in full, plus word searches over the whole 568,794-character OCR layer; OCR-based absence is weaker evidence than a text-layer absence, so each claim is also consistent with the contents list). No Pythagoras theorem (Питагор: 0 hits). No circle circumference or area — the circle lessons cover arcs, tangents and central angles only (обиколка, circumference: 0 hits; "плоштина на круг", area of a circle: 0 hits). No similarity and no congruence as topics: подударност (congruence) has 0 hits and сличност appears once, in an everyday phrase about positions; congruent triangles are mentioned only descriptively ("same shape and size") inside parallelogram properties. No inequalities as a topic (неравенство/нееднаквост: 0 hits) — ≥ and ≤ appear only inside contexts. No systems of equations and no quadratic equations (квадратна occurs only as the adjective "square" for a room and a board). No trigonometry (0 hits), no logarithms, no standard form. Volume is cuboid and cube only: prism, cylinder, pyramid and cone appear solely in the projections lesson and a pyramid-net task, never for volume or surface area. No negative indices or fractional indices (powers stop at natural exponents, roots at square roots to 100). Probability occupies only the final two lessons and stays at equally likely outcomes plus experiment — no sample-space diagrams or tree diagrams. There is no answers section: worked examples carry the checking.

**Confirmed book errors** (2). Each was first suspected from the OCR layer, then verified against the rendered printed page and recomputed; eight further OCR suspects (printed pp. 47, 53, 56, 115 bus division, 171, 257, 282, 295) were checked on renders and found to be printed correctly — they were OCR misreadings, and two of them (p. 47: (−305) − (−227) = −78; p. 257: x = 5.5 read back into the story) are correct in ways the OCR had made look wrong.

- **E1 — printed p. 113, "Множење и делење на децимални броеви" (Multiplying and dividing decimal numbers), Example 2.** Reducing 3.26 × 1.8 to whole numbers, the book prints "(3,26 · 100) · (1,8 · 10) = 326 · 19 = 5868". But 1.8 · 10 = 18, not 19 — and 326 · 19 = 6194, not 5868. The printed product 5868 is right; the printed factor 19 is the slip. Teach corrected: "Multiply the decimals as whole numbers: 326 · 18 = 5868. The two factors together have 3 decimal places, so 3.26 × 1.8 = 5.868."
- **E2 — printed p. 231, "Транслација во координатен систем" (Translation in a coordinate system), worked example.** The example moves two packages and states the results: A(2, 3) goes to A₁(8, 5) and B(5, 4) goes to B₁(11, 6) — a shift of +6 in x and +2 in y, which the accompanying diagram also shows. But the rule stated in part б) says "На x-координатата на секој пакет додаваме 3" (to the x-coordinate of each package we add 3) and 2 to y. Under +3/+2 the images would be A₁(5, 5) and B₁(8, 6), not the printed points. The stated rule and the stated results contradict each other on the same page. Teach corrected (keeping the printed results and diagram): "Translation adds the same amounts to every point: add 6 to the x-coordinate and 2 to the y-coordinate — A(2, 3) → A₁(8, 5), B(5, 4) → B₁(11, 6). Translation preserves distances."

Beyond these, all worked examples recomputed during the read — several dozen across the five themes — were correct, including the distributive-law check to −1666 (p. 55), the associativity check 2322 both ways (p. 56), the angle borrowing subtraction (p. 171), the micron stacking (p. 282) and the day-plan durations (p. 295).

**Held-copy defects.** The reduced copy is complete. Its 386 PDF pages match the CIP extent exactly under the verified offset: printed pp. 1–384, with printed p. 384 a blank final leaf (PDF p. 386, uniformly white in the pixel scan) and PDF pp. 2 and 6 likewise the book's own blank leaves around the front matter — no content page is dropped, duplicated or illegible anywhere in the copy (pixel-variance scan of all 386 renders plus the page-by-page OCR read; every contents entry was found at its printed page). The reduction's only costs are those of any reduced copy: fine print and small diagram labels are at the edge of legibility at 150 dpi (the colophon's design credit is the one line that could not be read with full confidence), and the PDF is print-restricted by its encryption — neither affects the read. The metadata's 2026 dates are a distiller artefact of the reduction and are not the book's date.

*Citations for this book: North Macedonia + Математика 7 + Grade 7 + page + 2025.*

### Citation check — Grade 7 (2026-11-07, checker ≠ reader)

20 anchors checked on exact PDF pages — **20 passed, 0 corrected, 0 not found**; 11 placement lines spot-verified, no page wrong. Special claims verified on fresh 150-dpi renders: both filed errors (printed p. 113 "326 · 19 = 5868" — the factor must be 18, since 326 × 18 = 5868 and 326 × 19 = 6194; printed p. 231's rule line contradicting its own +6/+2 translation results); three of the eight OCR suspects re-judged printed-correct (printed pp. 47, 171, 295); the colophon identity (АЛБИ Скопје 2025; ISBN 978-608-241-054-8; approval 26-913/1 од 22.08.2025; CIP extent 384 pp.; COBISS.MK-ID 66752517); completeness — an independent pixel scan found exactly PDF pp. 2, 6, 386 uniformly blank, the reduced copy is complete, and the offset (+2) held at 17 fresh folio sightings.

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 8 (PARTIAL filing — first North Macedonia maths section)

> **Edition warning, stated plainly:** this is the **2010 edition** — Jovo Stefanovski & Dr Naum Celakoski, *Математика за осмо одделение: осумгодишно основно образование* (Mathematics for Grade 8, eight-year primary education), Skopje 2010. It is **NOT the current edition**. The held file is a Wayback capture dated 2017 of the ministry e-ucebnici PDF of this 2010 book. Every citation from this book must carry the year **2010**.

### Book facts (exactly as printed)

- File: `north-macedonia-g8-matematika-mak-wayback2017.pdf` — **219 PDF pages, 8,957,122 bytes**, %PDF-1.6.
- Source (MANIFEST.md #7): Wayback capture `web/20170118010441id_/http://www.e-ucebnici.mon.gov.mk/pdf/Matematika_8_mak.pdf`.
- Title page (PDF p. 1): authors **JOVO STEFANOVSKI / NAUM CELAKOSKI**, "2010 / Skopje", and the title wording in a display font (checker-corrected from a render — the text layer does not extract the display font): "МАТЕМАТИКА / ОСМО ОДДЕЛЕНИЕ / Осумгодишно основно образование" and "ДЕВЕТТО ОДДЕЛЕНИЕ / Деветгодишно основно образование".
- Colophon (PDF p. 219, sight-read as a rendered image):
  - Authors: Јово Стефановски, Д-р Наум Целакоски (Jovo Stefanovski, Dr Naum Celakoski).
  - Reviewers: Д-р Јорданка Митевска (PMF – Skopje); Жанета Шумкоска; Агим Букла.
  - Editor of the edition: Јово Стефановски. Language editor: Сузана Стојковска. Computer processing & design: Драган Шопкоски.
  - Publisher: **Министерство за образование и наука за Република Македонија** (Ministry of Education and Science of the Republic of Macedonia — the state's name as printed in 2010, before "North Macedonia").
  - Printer: Графички центар дооел, Скопје. Print run: **16.600**.
  - Use approval, as printed in the colophon: "Со решение на Министерот за образование и наука на Република Македонија бр. **22-2321/1 од 21.04.2010** година се одобрува употребата на овој учебник" (by decision of the Minister № 22-2321/1 of 21.04.2010, use of this textbook is approved).
  - CIP: 373.3.016:51 (075.2)=163.3 — "Математика за осмо одделение : осумгодишно основно образование / Јово Стефановски, Наум Целакоски. – Скопје : Министерство за образование и наука на Република Македонија, **2010**. – **219 стр.** : илустр. ; 25 см".
  - **ISBN 978-608-4575-88-7**. COBISS.MK-ID 84078858.
- Language: Macedonian (Cyrillic). The PDF text layer is a legacy-font encoding that extracts as Latin transliteration; all content below was read from that full text layer (563,638 extracted characters, all 219 pages) and cross-checked against rendered images for the title page, the Pythagoras lesson (printed p. 42) and both closing imprint pages.
- Structure aids printed in the book: every teaching unit uses the same furniture — *Потсети се!* (Recall!), lettered portions A/B/…, *Запомни!* (Remember!), *Треба да знаеш:* (You should know), *Провери се!* (Check yourself), *Задачи* (Tasks), *Обиди се!* (Try it — optional), and a whole-theme test *Провери го твоето знаење* (Check your knowledge) at the end of each theme. Answers and solutions to the tasks are printed at printed pp. 209–215; a glossary/index of terms (*Преглед на поими*) is at printed pp. 216–218.

### Page offset — verified

**Printed folio = PDF page** on every numbered content page checked (folios read from page footers across all four themes, e.g. PDF p. 4 = printed 4, PDF p. 42 = printed 42, PDF p. 122 = printed 122, PDF p. 209 = printed 209, PDF p. 216 = printed 216, PDF p. 217 = printed 217). Two exceptions at the very end, both defects of the printed book/PDF assembly, not of the offset:

- PDF p. 218 **and** PDF p. 219 both carry the printed folio **218** (see Held-copy defects). Citations below therefore give both numbers: "PDF p. X = printed p. X" holds for pp. 3–217; the two closing imprint pages are cited by PDF page only.

Front matter: PDF p. 1 title page (unnumbered), PDF p. 2 "Драг ученику!" (Dear pupil — authors' introduction, unnumbered), PDF p. 3 = printed 3, Theme 1 contents.

### Everything taught (own summary)

- **Ratio and proportion (Theme 1, units 1–5).** Ratio of two numbers and of two segments; the value of a ratio; equal ratios, inverse ratio and continued ratio (a : b : c); commensurable and incommensurable segments (the side and diagonal of a square, whose ratio is √2 — stated, not developed). Proportion and its terms; finding an unknown term; proportional segments and the coefficient of proportionality; the geometric mean of two segments (a : x = x : b, x² = ab; sight-read example: 6 is the geometric mean of 4 and 9, printed p. 10). Dividing a segment into equal parts and in a given ratio by construction. **Thales' theorem** on proportional segments, with its converse, and its form inside a triangle; construction of the fourth proportional.
- **Similarity (Theme 1, units 6–9).** Similar figures and similar triangles defined by equal corresponding angles and proportional corresponding sides, similarity coefficient k. First criterion (two equal angles, AA), second and third criteria (proportional sides with included angle; three proportional sides). Perimeters of similar triangles are in the ratio of corresponding sides; **areas are in the ratio of the squares** of corresponding sides (printed p. 35).
- **Right-triangle similarity and Pythagoras (Theme 1, units 10–12).** Euclid's theorems for the right-angled triangle: each leg is the geometric mean of the hypotenuse and its projection on it (a² = cp, b² = cq), and the altitude to the hypotenuse is the geometric mean of the two projections (h² = pq), printed p. 38. **Pythagoras' theorem** stated and proved, with its converse (if a² + b² = c² the triangle is right-angled); finding any side from the other two; applications (diagonals, altitudes, distances, shadow/ladder problems); Pythagorean triples, including the generating forms in the optional material at printed p. 44. Sight-read worked example (printed p. 42): diagonal of a rectangle with sides 6 dm and 11 cm — converted to 60 cm and 11 cm, d² = 60² + 11² = 3721, d = 61 cm (correct; see Confirmed book errors for the one defect on this same page).
- **Population and sample (Theme 1, unit 13).** Population vs sample; why sampling is used; a sample must be **representative**, chosen **randomly**; judging given sampling plans (the bus-stop survey is criticised as unrepresentative); reading survey tables and carrying a sample percentage back to the whole population (e.g. a sample that is 10% of the population, printed pp. 50–51).
- **Linear equations in one unknown (Theme 2, units 1–7).** Equality, equation, unknown, domain of an equation; an **identity** (true for every value) vs a contradictory/impossible equation (true for none), printed p. 58. Equations classified by number of unknowns and by degree — linear (1st), quadratic (2nd), cubic (3rd) are **named only** here. Solution/root; checking a value; **equivalent equations** and the two theorems (add/subtract the same number or expression to both sides; multiply/divide both sides by a non-zero number) with their corollaries (moving terms across, clearing denominators). General form **ax + b = 0** with the three cases (a ≠ 0: unique solution x = −b/a; a = 0, b ≠ 0: no solution; a = 0, b = 0: infinitely many solutions). Word-problem applications with a printed check step.
- **Linear inequalities in one unknown (Theme 2, units 8–12).** Inequality statement vs inequality in an unknown; solution set; **intervals** (open, closed, half-open, infinite rays) and number-line representation; basic inequalities in solved form; theorems on equivalent inequalities, including **reversing the sign when multiplying or dividing by a negative number**; solving linear inequalities; a **system of two linear inequalities** solved as the intersection of the two solution intervals, including the no-solution (empty intersection) case.
- **Linear functions (Theme 2, units 13–17).** Linear function **y = kx + n**: argument and coefficient; the **zero of the function** (the x-value where y = 0, printed p. 106). Graphing from a table of values; checking whether a point lies on the graph; graphs with equal k are **parallel**; graphs with equal n meet the y-axis at the same point (0, n); a function is **increasing** for k > 0, **decreasing** for k < 0, constant for k = 0; solving a linear equation graphically as the x-intercept of its graph.
- **Random events and statistical probability (Theme 2, unit 18).** Certain, impossible and possible (random) events; equally likely outcomes; probability as a number between 0 and 1 (also as %). **Statistical probability** from experiment: sight-read table (printed p. 121) of five series of 100 coin tosses giving relative frequencies 0.52, 0.49, 0.53, … settling near 0.5. (The formula-based **mathematical probability** V(A) = m/n is taught later, in Theme 4 unit 15.)
- **Systems of two linear equations (Theme 3, units 1–7).** A linear equation in two unknowns, ax + by = c; its solution as an **ordered pair**, with infinitely many solutions shown on its graph line. Equivalent equations in two unknowns. A **system** of two such equations and its solution as the common ordered pair. Graphical solving with the three cases (one solution / no solution / infinitely many). Solving by the **substitution method** (unit 5) and by the **opposite-coefficients (elimination) method** (unit 6). Applications: number, money/coin, age and mixture problems (the mixture lesson works with % concentrations, printed p. 150).
- **Dirichlet's principle (Theme 3, unit 8).** The pigeonhole principle used as a proof method: sight-read examples — in a class of 30 pupils whose test errors fall into 9 possible counts (0–8), at least 4 pupils share the same count (30 = 3·9 + 3, printed pp. 154–155); birthdays, initials, divisibility of differences and angle arguments.
- **Space geometry foundations (Theme 4, units 1–5).** Point, line and plane in space; coplanar points/lines; two lines — intersecting, parallel, coincident or **skew**; two planes — intersecting (in a line) or parallel; a line and a plane; a line **perpendicular** to a plane and its piercing point. **Parallel projection and orthogonal projection** of a point, segment and figure onto a plane. Drawing solids (cube, cuboid) on paper.
- **Prism (Theme 4, units 6–9).** Prism and its elements (bases, lateral faces/edges, height); kinds (right/oblique, regular, n-gonal); diagonal sections. Parallelepiped, cuboid and cube, including the cuboid's space diagonal. The **net** of a right prism and its **surface area P = 2B + M** (B base area, M lateral area). **Volume axioms** (positive; congruent solids have equal volumes; volumes add over parts; unit cube), printed pp. 184–185; volume of a cuboid **V = abc** and of a cube **V = a³**; also V = B·H; cubic units and the litre (1 l = 1 dm³). Volume of any right prism, **V = B·H**.
- **Pyramid, cylinder, cone, sphere (Theme 4, units 10–14).** Pyramid and its elements (including the apothem of a regular pyramid); surface area P = B + M; **volume V = ⅓B·H** (printed p. 194). Cylinder: bases, axis, axial section, net; **P = 2Rπ(R + H)**, **V = R²πH**. Cone: generatrix s with s² = H² + R²; net as circle + circular sector; **P = Rπ(R + s)**, **V = ⅓R²πH**. Sphere (surface) vs ball (solid), great circle; **P = 4R²π**, **V = ⁴⁄₃R³π**, with the book's derivation V = ⅓PR (printed p. 204).
- **Mathematical probability (Theme 4, unit 15).** For equally likely outcomes, **V(A) = m/n** (favourable over all possible cases), printed pp. 206–207, applied to dice, cards and spinners; an impossible event has probability 0 and a certain event 1 in the same notation.

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 8 book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the four printed contents pages plus full-text searches of the extracted text layer (563,638 characters) for the relevant Macedonian terms, all returning zero or name-only hits as noted.

- **No quadratic equations are solved** — no quadratic formula, no factorising quadratics, no vertex/parabola work. "Квадратна" (quadratic) occurs only where equations are *classified by degree* (Theme 2 unit 2, and the glossary index entries pointing to pp. 60/86); the graph drawn for every function in this book is a straight line.
- **No trigonometry** — no sine, cosine or tangent of an angle anywhere (full-text search: 0 hits for the terms). Right-angled triangles are handled only by similarity, Euclid's theorems and Pythagoras.
- **No circle theorems** — no inscribed/central angle theorems, no tangent properties, no chord theorems. Circles appear only as bases of cylinders/cones, as the great circle of a sphere, and via circumference/area facts used inside the solids formulas.
- **No sequences or series as a topic** — no arithmetic/geometric progressions (the linear function is never re-framed as an arithmetic sequence).
- **No powers/indices or roots as topics** — no index laws, no square-root arithmetic as a skill (√ appears inside Pythagoras/Euclid results), no standard form, and **no negative-numbers topic** (negatives occur inside equations/inequalities only).
- **No statistics beyond sampling and probability** — no mean/median/mode, no spread, no data charts or graphs as a taught skill (survey results stay in tables); probability is the statistical (relative-frequency) form in Theme 2 and the m/n form in Theme 4, with no combined-events rules.
- **No vectors, matrices, logarithms, complex numbers, or calculus** — as expected at this grade, recorded for completeness of the filing.
- **No transformations beyond projection** — no translation/rotation/reflection/enlargement strand; similarity is treated through triangles and Thales, not as a transformation, and congruence appears only in passing (congruent solids in the volume axioms; congruent triangles inside similarity work).

### Confirmed book errors

1. **Printed p. 42 (PDF p. 42), Theme 1 unit 11, task 6б — a side is named with the wrong letter (confirmed by sight-reading the rendered page).** The task prints: "Страните на ΔABC се: а) a = 7, b = 24, c = 25; б) **c = 8, b = 10, c = 15**. Провери дали ΔABC е правоаголен." ("The sides of ΔABC are: a) a = 7, b = 24, c = 25; b) **c = 8, b = 10, c = 15**. Check whether ΔABC is right-angled.") The letter **c is used twice and a never appears**, so as printed the three sides cannot be read off. **Teach-corrected wording:** read the first value as side **a = 8** — i.e. sides 8, 10, 15; the corrected triple is *not* right-angled (8² + 10² = 164 ≠ 225 = 15²), and part a) *is* right-angled (7² + 24² = 625 = 25²), so the exercise's yes/no contrast survives the correction.
   - Explicit non-error on the same page, checked so it is not "corrected" by mistake: task B7's worked solution for a rectangle with sides **a = 6 dm and b = 11 cm** converts 6 dm to 60 cm *before* applying Pythagoras (d² = 60² + 11² = 3721, d = 61 cm) — the arithmetic and the unit conversion are both correct.
2. No other confirmed errors. All other worked examples, "Запомни!" formulas and printed answers that were recomputed during the read checked out, including: the geometric-mean value 6 for 4 and 9 (printed p. 10); h² = 41² − 9² = 1600, h = 40 (printed p. 43); the answers key values for Theme 1 unit 11 tasks 1 (37; 33; ≈ 40) at printed p. 209; the cuboid/cube and prism volume formulas and the sphere formulas P = 4R²π, V = ⁴⁄₃R³π (printed pp. 185, 188, 204); and the coin-toss relative frequencies approaching 0.5 (printed p. 121).

### Held-copy defects

- **Duplicated final folio.** PDF p. 218 and PDF p. 219 **both carry the printed folio 218**. PDF p. 218 is a sparse publisher page (publisher's name/address, the three reviewers, and the ministerial approval sentence); PDF p. 219 is the full colophon. The book is not missing its colophon — it carries both pages.
- **Blank approval fields on PDF p. 218.** On the publisher page the approval sentence reads "Со Решение на Министерот за образование и наука на Република Македонија број [blank] од [blank] година, се одобрува употребата на овој учебник" — decision number and date left empty. The filled-in approval (№ 22-2321/1 of 21.04.2010) is on the colophon, PDF p. 219; cite the colophon, not PDF p. 218, for the approval.
- **Blank versos.** PDF pp. 54 and 126 are blank (the versos of the Theme 1 and Theme 2 test pages). No content is missing: each theme's test is complete on its recto and the next theme's contents page follows.
- **Legacy-font text layer.** The PDF's embedded text extracts as Latin transliteration of the Cyrillic (a legacy encoding), so the file cannot be searched as Cyrillic; content claims in this draft were cross-checked against rendered images for the title page, printed p. 42 and both closing imprint pages. All 219 PDF pages render; the book is complete from title page through glossary and colophon.

*Filing status: PARTIAL — Grade 1 and Grade 8 read for North Macedonia; the country's other 10 in-hand maths books remain unread (READING-QUEUE row 62). Citations for this book: North Macedonia + Математика за осмо одделение + Grade 8 + page + **2010** (older edition — never cite as the current edition).*

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 11 (gymnasium year II)

> Book: *МАТЕМАТИКА II — учебник за втора година гимназиско образование* (Mathematics II — textbook for the second year of gymnasium education), Д-р Верица Бакева Смиљкова & проф. Горан Михајлоски (Dr Verica Bakeva Smilkova & Prof. Goran Mihajloski), АЛБИ (Albi), Skopje **2026**, first edition. A current, newly approved textbook — the newest book in the North Macedonia set.

### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF is **image-only**: `pdftotext` extracts 332 characters (form feeds only) from the whole file, so nothing could be machine-read. The book was read **visually from page renders at 100 dpi**:

- the **complete contents** (printed pp. 5–7) — the full topic map below is transcribed from the book's own contents, in the book's order, with the book's printed page numbers;
- the **front matter in full** (cover, title page, colophon, preface — PDF pp. 1–6);
- **sight-read lesson pages across all six themes**, on which the summaries and the error check rest: Theme 1 opener and unit 1.1 (printed p. 8), unit 1.9 number sets (p. 50), unit 1.10 complex addition/subtraction (p. 57), unit 2.4 discriminant discussion (p. 90), unit 3.7 quadratic inequalities (p. 141), unit 4.9 classical probability (p. 193), unit 5.2 acute-angle trigonometry (p. 213), unit 5.7 space-geometry axioms (p. 231), unit 6.6 pyramid area/volume (p. 290), and the closing Literature page (p. 330).

Not claimed: a line-by-line read of every exercise on all 330 printed pages. Claims below rest on the contents map + the sight-read pages named above; the negatives are contents-based, as flagged there.

### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Powers and roots (Theme 1, units 1.1–1.8).** Powers and the power laws first (the theme opens by defining aⁿ as a repeated product, base and exponent named, printed p. 8), then the root concept, rational exponents with a root rewritten as a power, expanding/reducing roots to a common index, root of a product and of a quotient, adding/subtracting like roots, all four operations on roots, and irrational expressions closed by rationalising denominators.
- **Extending the number sets (unit 1.9, sight-read p. 50).** The chain N ⊂ Z ⊂ Q ⊂ R is built by need: N is closed under + and × but 2 − 5 ∉ N and 6 + x = 3 has no solution in N, so 0 and the negatives are adjoined (Z); in Z the equation 2x = 5 still fails, so the rationals Q = {a/b | a,b ∈ Z, b ≠ 0} are adjoined; x² = 5 has no rational solution, so the irrationals are adjoined — √2 = 1.41421356…, √3 = 1.7320508…, π = 3.14159265… (all three decimal expansions checked correct). The book denotes the irrationals by **J** ("Множеството на ирационални броеви најчесто се означува со J") — a distinctive national notation to file as taught.
- **Complex numbers (units 1.10–1.13).** Motivated in the theme introduction as the extension that lets equations with no real solution be solved. Addition and subtraction are defined component-wise (sight-read p. 57: z₁ + z₂ = (a + c) + (b + d)i, z₁ − z₂ = (a − c) + (b − d)i; the recall box first rehearses i as the imaginary unit with i² = −1 and the conjugate; all three worked sums/differences on the page recomputed correct, e.g. (4 − 2i) + 2i = 4, (3 − 6i) − 5 = −2 − 6i). Multiplication, powers of a complex number, division (via the conjugate), and application tasks close the strand. Only the algebraic form a + bi is used (see Honest negatives).
- **Quadratic equations (Theme 2).** Concept and kinds; incomplete equations solved directly; complete equations by the formula; **discussion of the solutions** by the discriminant (sight-read p. 90: D = b² − 4ac; the worked triple x² + 2x − 15 = 0 (D = 64, roots 3 and −5), x² + 2x + 1 = 0 (D = 0, double root −1), x² + 2x + 5 = 0 (D = −16 < 0, no real solutions) — all recomputed correct; the summary box states D ≥ 0 ⟺ real solutions). Vieta's formulas; composing an equation from given solutions and factorising the quadratic trinomial; fractional rational equations; biquadratic equations; and a mixed system (one linear + one quadratic equation in two unknowns).
- **Quadratic function and inequalities (Theme 3).** The parabola is built in steps: f(x) = x², then ax² and ax² + c, then the canonical (vertex) form y = a(x − α)² + β, then the full ax² + bx + c; properties; a full "course and graph" sketch routine. Quadratic inequalities are solved **algebraically**: factorise the trinomial into linear factors and split the product's sign condition into two systems of linear inequalities (sight-read p. 141: x² + 2x − 8 < 0, zeros −4 and 2 from x₁/₂ = (−2 ± √(4 + 32))/2, written (x + 4)(x − 2) < 0 and continued as two linear systems — the factorisation method, not a sign chart, is the book's first way). Systems of quadratic inequalities in one unknown close the theme.
- **Counting and probability (Theme 4).** Variations with and without repetition, permutations, combinations, then combined tasks — counting is taught **before** probability and with no binomial theorem (see negatives). Experiments and events; the set of elementary events Ω with a random event as a subset, |A| ≤ |Ω|; operations with events; the **classical definition** (sight-read p. 193: P(A) = k/n with k the number of elements in A and n the number in Ω, for equally likely elementary events — dice, coin, drawing a ball); properties of probability and independent events.
- **Trigonometry of the acute angle (Theme 5, units 5.1–5.6).** Angles and their measurement, then the trigonometric functions of an acute angle **defined through similarity**: normals dropped from points on one arm of the angle give similar right triangles, so the ratios BC/AB etc. are constant for the angle (sight-read p. 213: sin α = opposite leg / hypotenuse, cos α = adjacent leg / hypotenuse, stated as "Запомни!" boxes). Functions of complementary angles; relations between the functions of the same angle; simplifying trigonometric expressions; applications.
- **Space geometry (Theme 5, units 5.7–5.12).** The strand opens axiomatically (sight-read p. 231: concepts that are not defined are **basic concepts** — in geometry point, line and plane; concepts defined from them are derived/defined concepts; the preface adds the explicit theorem-vs-axiom distinction). Mutual positions of lines and planes in space; then prism, pyramid, cylinder and cone, each with its **sections by a plane**.
- **Measurement (Theme 6).** Perimeter and area in the plane: square/rectangle, parallelogram, triangle, trapezium and deltoid (kite), circle and parts of a circle. Then surface area and volume of solids: pyramid (sight-read p. 290: P = B + M; worked example — regular quadrilateral pyramid, base edge 10 cm, lateral edge 13 cm: B = 100 cm², apothem from s² = h² + (a/2)², h² = 169 − 25 = 144, h = 12 cm, M = 2·a·h = 240 cm², P = 340 cm² — recomputed correct), truncated pyramid, cone, truncated cone, surface area of a sphere and parts of a sphere, volume of a ball and its parts.

### Honest negatives — what a gymnasium year-II book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (printed pp. 5–7) contains no entry for any of the following, and none appeared in the sight-read pages. (Contents-based negatives; a stray mention inside an unread exercise cannot be excluded, but no topic below is *taught* anywhere in the book.)

- **No trigonometric or polar form of a complex number** — complex numbers stay in the algebraic form a + bi; no modulus/argument geometry, no De Moivre. (That material lives in the Grade 12 elective Algebra book, §6.2–6.4.)
- **No binomial theorem and no Pascal's triangle** — combinations are taught (unit 4.4) but the contents has no binomial-formula unit.
- **No logarithms or exponential functions** — powers stop at rational exponents; no exponential/logarithmic function or equation anywhere in the contents.
- **No sequences or progressions** — no arithmetic/geometric sequence unit.
- **No vectors, matrices or determinants**, no coordinate/analytic geometry of the line or circle — geometry is synthetic (space axioms, solids, sections) plus measurement.
- **No statistics** — no data handling, mean or charts; probability is counting + the classical definition only, with no addition/multiplication rules named beyond "properties of probability; independent events" (unit 4.10) and no conditional probability in the contents.
- **No circle theorems and no similarity/congruence strand** — plane geometry in this book is angles, acute-angle trigonometry and area; similarity appears only inside the derivation of the trigonometric ratios (unit 5.2).
- **No answers section** — unlike the three Grade 12 books, this book prints no answers/solutions to its tasks.

### Confirmed book errors

**None found in the pages sight-read.** Every worked computation on the sight-read pages was recomputed and is correct, including: the decimal expansions √2 = 1.41421356…, √3 = 1.7320508…, π = 3.14159265… (printed p. 50); the three complex sums/differences of unit 1.10 Example 1 (printed p. 57); the discriminant triple D = 64 / 0 / −16 with roots 3, −5 / double −1 / none (printed p. 90); the zeros −4 and 2 of x² + 2x − 8 (printed p. 141); and the pyramid computation B = 100, h = 12, M = 240, P = 340 cm² (printed p. 290). Two wording slips were noted in the **contents only** (not in teaching text): the contents line for unit 3.3 says "квадратна равенка" (quadratic *equation*) where the quadratic *function* is meant — the Theme 3 heading itself reads КВАДРАТНА ФУНКЦИЈА correctly *(corrected at citation check — an earlier draft also blamed the Theme 3 heading)*. The authors' own preface (printed p. 3–4) warns that technical errors may remain in this first edition and will be corrected in the next — recorded here so a checker treats any later find against that warning. Because the read was contents + front matter + the sight-read lesson pages named above, this is "none found in the pages sight-read", not a guarantee about every exercise on all 330 pages.

### Held-copy defects

- None affecting content: all 332 PDF pages render; the book is complete from the cover (PDF p. 1) through Literature (printed p. 330 = PDF p. 332), and the CIP extent (330 pp.) matches the last numbered page. PDF p. 2 is blank, as printed.
- The file is **image-only** (no text layer at all — 332 extracted characters), so the book cannot be text-searched or copy-checked by machine; folio numbers sit in coloured theme bands at the page foot and are legible at 100 dpi.
- The held PDF has **no back cover** (the PDF ends at the Literature page); nothing in the contents or CIP points to content after p. 330, so no teaching content is missing.

*Citation for this book: North Macedonia + Математика II (учебник за втора година гимназиско образование) + Grade 11 (gymnasium year II) + page + 2026.*

19 anchors checked on exact PDF pages — **18 passed, 1 corrected, 0 not found**, all from fresh 100-dpi renders (the PDF is image-only). The correction: the errors section blamed the Theme 3 heading for the "квадратна равенка" slip — the heading reads КВАДРАТНА ФУНКЦИЈА correctly; only the contents line for unit 3.3 carries the slip (wording fixed at assembly). ## NORTH MACEDONIA — Mathematics, Grade 12 (gymnasium year III)

> Book: *МАТЕМАТИКА ЗА III ГОДИНА НА РЕФОРМИРАНОТО ГИМНАЗИСКО ОБРАЗОВАНИЕ* (Mathematics for the third year of the reformed gymnasium education), Јорданка Митевска, Иван Трајков, Лилјана Грибовска-Поповиќ (Jordanka Mitevska, Ivan Trajkov, Liljana Gribovska-Popovik), Просветно дело АД (Prosvetno Delo AD), Skopje — **second edition (II издание), 2018 printing**; the compulsory-subject Grade 12 book. The library's `full-year-alt.pdf`; the true Grade 12 Mathematics book (set-verification.md correction 1).

### Book facts (exactly as printed)

- File: `pdfs/g12-math.pdf` — **272 PDF pages, 44,941,919 bytes**; grabbed from Telegram (@liqlearns_textbooks msg 941, filename "grade-12__Matematika_za_treta_godina_MKD_vnatre.pdf"); SHA-256 059f33910e0ec38ab828dd480cd82e675d6ac6dc5e619b99511a443de8681ccc, equal to the phase3-staging copy.
