### Page offset — verified

**PDF page = printed page + 1** throughout. Spot-verified at: PDF p. 4 = printed 3 (preface); PDF p. 5 = printed 4 and PDF p. 6 = printed 5 (contents); PDF p. 7 = printed 6 (motivational opener with the fruit-fly exponential model); PDF p. 8 = printed 7 (unit 1.1.1 — matches the contents entry "7"); PDF pp. 19, 26, 102, 126, 143, 154, 173, 198, 210, 231 = printed 18, 25, 101, 125, 142, 153, 172, 197, 209, 230 (each a contents-listed unit start); PDF p. 244 = printed 243 (Answers head); PDF p. 268 = printed 267 (index); PDF p. 270 = printed 269 (colophon); PDF p. 272 = printed 271 (CIP, last page).

### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF is a scan with a **decorative-only text layer**: `pdftotext` returns 32,587 characters, almost all of it the letters of the cover design and of the diagonal publisher watermark spelled out letter by letter; no teaching text extracts. The book was therefore read **visually from page renders at 100 dpi**:

- the **complete contents** (printed pp. 4–5) — the full topic map below is transcribed from the book's own contents, in the book's order, with the book's printed page numbers;
- the **front and back matter in full** (cover, title page, imprint/approval page, preface, answers head, index head, colophon, CIP page);
- **sight-read lesson pages in every theme**: the exponential-model opener (printed p. 6), unit 1.1.1 (p. 7), the logarithm concept (p. 18), change of base (p. 25), basic trigonometric equations (p. 101), the combinatorics theme opener with mathematical induction (p. 125), the binomial formula (p. 142), the classical definition of probability (p. 153), the line in the plane (p. 172), second-order curves and the circle (p. 197), the ellipse (p. 209), the parabola (p. 230).

Not claimed: a line-by-line read of every exercise on all 271 printed pages. Claims below rest on the contents map + the sight-read pages named above; the negatives are contents-based, as flagged there.

### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Exponential function (Theme 1).** The theme is motivated before it is defined: a frontispiece page (printed p. 6, "ПРИРОДА. ЖИВОТ. МАТЕМАТИКА.") models a fruit-fly population by y = ae^(bt) from 100 flies after two days and 300 after four, solving the system 100 = ae^(2b), 300 = ae^(4b) to get b = ½ ln 3 ≈ 0.5493, a = 100/3, and ≈ 520 flies after five days (recomputed correct) — and tells the pupil the tools to do this are what the theme teaches. The power concept is then extended to a real exponent, the exponential function f(x) = aˣ is graphed and read (course), and exponential equations follow.
- **Logarithm (Theme 1).** The logarithm is introduced as the inverse operation met when solving aˣ = b: log_a b = x ⟺ aˣ = b, with the basic identity a^(log_a b) = b (sight-read p. 18; immediate consequences log_a 1 = 0, log_a a = 1, log_a aⁿ = n; 0 and negatives have no real logarithm). Log rules; **change of base** (sight-read p. 25: log_a b = log_c b / log_c a, proved from the basic identity; the corollary log_a b = 1/log_b a; worked computations log₈₁ 3 = 1/4 and log₃₂ ∛2 = 1/15 and a telescoping chain log₃5·log₅7·log₇11·log₁₁13·log₁₃15 = 1 + log₃5 — all recomputed correct). Graph of the logarithmic function; logarithmic equations.
- **Trigonometry (Theme 2).** The angle concept is extended (general angle, degree and radian measure), the functions are defined for an arbitrary angle and on the trigonometric circle, signs by quadrant, special-angle values, relations between the functions, computing all values from one, basic properties (periodicity), and reduction to an acute angle. Graphs: the basic functions, then y = a sin(bx + c) + d and y = a cos(bx + c) + d with amplitude/period/phase read off (the index confirms "амплитуда на функција 81"). Transforming expressions: addition theorems, double- and half-angle functions, product-to-sum and sum-to-product. **Basic trigonometric equations** (sight-read p. 101: sin x = m has no solution for |m| > 1; for |m| ≤ 1 the two solutions in a period are read off the unit circle, x₁ = π − x₀ etc.), then reducible equations — with the methodological warning, stated in the text, that transformations such as squaring both sides can introduce new roots, so a found solution must be checked by substitution in the original equation. The theme closes by **solving an arbitrary triangle**: sine rule, cosine rule, and practical applications (the index lists angles of depression and elevation, p. 119).
- **Combinatorics and probability (Theme 3).** Combinatorics opens, unusually, with **mathematical induction** (sight-read p. 125: induction presented as the method that proves statements for every natural number, expressly because the counting formulas that follow "can be derived with the help of mathematical induction"). Variations, permutations, combinations; the **binomial formula** built from row-by-row expansions (a + b)⁰…(a + b)⁵ and Pascal's triangle, whose symmetry and row-sum properties are listed (sight-read p. 142 — every printed expansion and triangle row checked correct). Probability: experiment and event, **statistical probability** (relative frequency) first, the set of random events, then the **classical definition** (sight-read p. 153: P(A) = p/n — note the book's letters are the reverse of the Grade 11 book's: here **p** counts the favourable and **n** all the elementary events; P(Ω) = 1, P(∅) = 0, 0 ≤ P(A) ≤ 1, P(Ā) = 1 − P(A); worked coin P = 1/2 and dice values 3/6 = 0.5 and 4/6 = 0.6666… recomputed correct).
- **Analytic geometry (Theme 4).** Points and vectors in a rectangular coordinate system, vector operations in coordinates, distance between two points, dividing a segment in a given ratio. The line: each position of a line is set by "determining parameters"; the explicit form is derived from tg α = (y − n)/x as y = x·tg α + n (sight-read p. 172), then general, point, two-point and intercept forms, intersection and angle of two lines, parallelism/perpendicularity conditions, normal form, distance from a point to a line. **Curves of the second order** are introduced as conic sections — sections of an upright cone by a plane — with the general second-degree equation Ax² + Bxy + Cy² + Dx + Ey + F = 0 (sight-read p. 197). Each conic is defined as a locus and carried through the same routine: circle K = {M(x, y) | S M̄ = r} (p. 197); ellipse by MF̄₁ + MF̄₂ = 2a, introduced with the string-and-pencil drawing construction (sight-read p. 209); hyperbola; parabola by MF̄ = MP̄ with focus, directrix and parameter p (sight-read p. 230) — and for each: mutual position with a line, and the equations of tangent and normal at a point of the curve.

### Honest negatives — what a Grade 12 (gymnasium year III) compulsory book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (printed pp. 4–5) contains no entry for any of the following, and none appeared in the sight-read pages. (Contents-based negatives, as above.)

- **No calculus** — no limits, derivatives or integrals anywhere; functions are studied by "тек" (course/behaviour) read from their graphs only.
- **No sequences or series** — no arithmetic/geometric progressions (the exponential function is never re-framed as a geometric sequence).
- **No complex numbers** — the set of numbers is never extended beyond R in this book (complex numbers live in the Grade 11 book and in the elective Algebra book).
- **No vectors in space and no analytic geometry in space** — vectors and coordinates are plane-only; the space treatment is in the elective Linear Algebra book.
- **No matrices or determinants**, no systems of linear equations as a topic.
- **No statistics** — no data, averages or charts; probability stops at the classical definition, with no addition/multiplication rules, no conditional probability and no probability distributions in the contents.
- **No inverse trigonometric functions as a topic** — angles are recovered graphically from the unit circle (unit 2.1.4), with no arcsin/arccos/arctan notation in the contents or the index.
- **No exponential or logarithmic inequalities** — only equations (units 1.1.2, 1.2.5); inequalities of these types are in the elective Algebra book (§5.8–5.9).

### Confirmed book errors

**None found in the pages sight-read.** Every worked computation on the sight-read pages was recomputed and is correct, including: the fruit-fly model b = ½ ln 3 ≈ 0.5493, a = 100/3, ≈ 520 flies at day five (printed p. 6); the change-of-base computations log₈₁ 3 = ¼, log₃₂ ∛2 = 1/15 and the telescoping product = 1 + log₃ 5 (printed p. 25); the expansions (a + b)⁰…(a + b)⁵ and Pascal's triangle rows (printed p. 142); the probability values ½, 3/6 = 0.5, 4/6 = 0.6666… (printed p. 153); and the answers-section values sight-read at printed p. 244 (log₂ 32 = 5; log₃ 1 = 0; log_√3 3 = 2). Two traps were checked and are **not** errors: (1) printed p. 18, Example 1 is a *check-the-correctness* exercise ("да ја проверите точноста на следните равенства") — of its five printed equalities, log₃ 27 = 2 and log_√2 4 = 2 are false **by design**, as items to be judged, while log₅ 5 = 1, log₁₆ 4 = ½ and log_{1/3} 9 = −2 are true; (2) the contents' page number for the Answers (242) against the section's actual start (243) is filed under Held-copy defects, not as a teaching error. Non-content slips noted: the title page prints the first author as "Јадранка" for "Јорданка" (cover, colophon and CIP agree on Јорданка); the contents prints 4.1.3's page as "166'" with a stray apostrophe. Because the read was contents + front/back matter + the sight-read lesson pages named above, this is "none found in the pages sight-read", not a guarantee about every exercise on all 271 pages.

### Held-copy defects

- **Blank page at the Answers boundary.** Printed p. 242 (PDF p. 243) carries only the folio and the publisher watermark — no content. The contents lists "ОДГОВОРИ И РЕШЕНИЈА НА ЗАДАЧИТЕ 242", but the Answers section head is on printed p. 243 (PDF p. 244) and the answers then run continuously. So either p. 242 is a printed blank divider and the contents number is the divider's page, or the contents is off by one; **no answers content is missing** — the section is present and complete from its head.
- **Every page carries the diagonal publisher watermark "PROSVETNO DELO"** across the page (a feature of this scan/publisher PDF, also in the other two Просветно дело books of this band); it overlays but does not hide the text at 100 dpi. The same watermark is the book's only "text layer" content together with the cover lettering.
- **CIP/edition mismatch (the book's own, not the copy's):** the CIP block (printed p. 271) records the 2003 first edition — 268 pp., ISBN 9989-0-0343-0 — while the held book is the second edition, printed September 2018, whose colophon gives the extent as 272 pages; the last printed folio is 271 and the PDF has 272 pages (cover + printed 1–271). Cite the year as **2018 (II издание)** with the CIP data noted, never as 2003.
- Otherwise complete: cover through CIP all render; contents, answers and index are all present; no duplicated or missing folios were found in the pages checked, and every contents-listed unit start sight-read sits at its listed page under the +1 offset.

*Citation for this book: North Macedonia + Математика за III година на реформираното гимназиско образование + Grade 12 (gymnasium year III) + page + 2018 (II издание; CIP 2003).*

Special claims verified: printed p. 242 is blank and the Answers begin on printed p. 243; the title page prints "Јадранка" where the cover, CIP and colophon print "Јорданка"; the offset (+1) at fresh folio sightings, including the Answers and the index ranges.

## NORTH MACEDONIA — Algebra, Grade 12 (gymnasium year III), elective

> Book: *АЛГЕБРА ЗА ТРЕТА ГОДИНА НА РЕФОРМИРАНОТО ГИМНАЗИСКО ОБРАЗОВАНИЕ (ИЗБОРЕН ПРЕДМЕТ)* (Algebra for the third year of the reformed gymnasium education — elective subject), Глигор Тренчевски, Костадин Тренчевски, Билјана Крстеска, Снежана Здравеска (Gligor Trenchevski, Kostadin Trenchevski, Biljana Krsteska, Snezana Zdraveska), Просветно дело АД, **fourth edition (IV издание)**; CIP record Skopje 2005. **The library's `grade-12/mathematics/student/full-year.pdf` is this book**: its books.json/TSV title "Mathematics — Grade 12" is wrong — the book is Algebra (set-verification.md correction 1). File it as Algebra.

The six are named: (1) Корени (Roots) — revision and deepening; (2) Одбрани делови од теорија на броеви (Selected parts of number theory) — divisibility criteria, and proofs of "некои критериуми за деливост, како и на теоремите на Ојлер, Ферма и Вилсон" (the theorems of Euler, Fermat and Wilson); (3) Неравенства (Inequalities); (4) Полиноми (Polynomials); (5) Равенки, системи равенки, неравенки и системи неравенки (Equations, systems of equations, inequalities and systems of inequalities) — sign of the quadratic trinomial read off a drawing; (6) Комплексни броеви (Complex numbers) — deepening the year-II material, reducing to the trigonometric form and operations in it. ### How this book was read — stated honestly

The PDF is a scan with a **decorative-only text layer** (24,629 extracted characters, cover/watermark lettering only). The book was read **visually from page renders at 100 dpi**, with one page (the Euler-function theorem) re-rendered at 200 dpi and cropped to confirm a statement's exact wording:

- the **complete contents** (printed pp. 186–187, at the back of the book) — the full topic map below is transcribed from it, in the book's order, with the book's printed page numbers;
- the **front and back matter in full** (cover, title page, imprint/approval page, preface and sign conventions, index end, contents, colophon);
- **sight-read lesson pages in every theme**: roots (printed pp. 5–7, the whole of unit 1.1's worked examples), the functions [x] and {x} (p. 25), Euler's function and its multiplicativity theorem (p. 37, re-checked at 200 dpi), the theorems of Euler, Fermat and Wilson (p. 39), absolute-value inequalities and the mean values (p. 58), Bezout's theorem (p. 76), undetermined coefficients (p. 90), partial fractions (p. 99), the forms of complex numbers (p. 153), the trigonometric form (p. 158), De Moivre's formula (p. 165), and the answers head (p. 177).

Not claimed: a line-by-line read of every exercise on all 188 printed pages. Claims below rest on the contents map + the sight-read pages named above; the negatives are contents-based, as flagged there.

### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Roots, deepened (Theme 1).** The revision goes well past school routine: roots are expanded/reduced to a common index and compared by it (sight-read p. 5: ∛3 = ⁶√9 > ⁶√8 = √2 because 9 > 8); roots of products and quotients are extracted by prime factorisation (√4356 = √(2²·3²·11²) = 66; √298116 = 546 — both recomputed correct); and the book stops to prove a negative: **"Значи нема правило за коренување на збир и разлика!"** — there is no rule for the root of a sum or difference — shown by √(8² + 6²) = 10 < 8 + 6 = 14 and √(10² − 6²) = 8 > 10 − 6 = 4 (printed p. 6). A standing note then fixes the convention that letters in identities are assumed to satisfy the conditions that make the expressions defined. Normal form of a root, operations, rationalising denominators, identities, simplifying irrational expressions, and **irrational equations** by two methods — a new unknown, and successive squaring.
- **Number theory (Theme 2) — the band's most distinctive strand.** The integer-part and fractional-part functions [x] and {x} come first, with real theorems proved (sight-read p. 25: for n coprime to 4, [n/4] + [2n/4] + [3n/4] = (3n − 3)/2, proved by the cases n = 4q + 1 and n = 4q + 3 — recomputed correct; the distinctness of the numbers {10ⁿ√3} proved from the irrationality of √3; a full list of floor properties including [x] + [−x] = 0 or −1). Then multiplicative functions; congruences in Z; complete and reduced systems of residues; **Euler's function** (sight-read p. 37: φ(m) counts the elements of a reduced residue system mod m; the printed table φ(1)…φ(12) = 1,1,2,2,4,2,6,4,6,4,10,4 — all correct; φ(p) = p − 1; multiplicativity — see Confirmed book errors for its statement); the **theorems of Euler, Fermat and Wilson, with proofs** (sight-read p. 39: Euler's theorem a^φ(m) ≡ 1 (mod m) proved by permuting a reduced system; Fermat's little theorem as its corollary, with the further corollary aᵖ ≡ a (mod p); worked application — 2³⁴⁰ − 1 is not prime because 2¹⁰ ≡ 1 (mod 11) gives 11 | 2³⁴⁰ − 1, recomputed correct); systems of linear congruences; Diophantine equations with Pythagorean triples; and **infinite descent**.
- **Inequalities (Theme 3).** Concept and kinds; absolute value with the full triangle-inequality family (sight-read p. 58: ||a| − |b|| ≤ |a + b| ≤ |a| + |b| and |a| − |b| ≤ |a − b|, i.e. |a − b| ≥ ||a| − |b|| — correct); inequalities with absolute values; the **four means** of positive numbers — quadratic (root-mean-square), arithmetic, geometric, harmonic, defined side by side — and the inequalities between them, proved (the sight-read page opens the proof of QM ≥ AM by fixing the sum and minimising); then "some more important inequalities".
- **Polynomials (Theme 4).** Concepts, addition/multiplication, division, divisibility — with **Bezout's theorem** (sight-read p. 76: the remainder of f(x) on division by x − c equals f(c), proved from f(x) ≡ (x − c)·q(x) + r; worked both ways for f(x) = x⁴ + 2x³ + 3x² − x + 1 ÷ (x + 2): f(−2) = 15 and long division gives quotient x³ + 3x − 7, remainder 15 — both recomputed correct). Zeros; **GCD of two polynomials** (Euclidean algorithm); integer/rational coefficients; the **method of undetermined coefficients** (sight-read p. 90: (x + 3)(x − 2)(x + 4) = x³ + 5x² − 2x − 24 found by evaluating at x = −3 and x = 2 and solving 3a − b = 17, 2a + b = 8; and a division 2x⁴ − 3x³ + 3x² + 8x − 1 ÷ (2x² + x − 1) carried out entirely by undetermined coefficients, q(x) = x² − 2x + 3, r(x) = 3x + 2 — both recomputed correct) together with the **fundamental theorem of algebra**; generalised Vieta relations between zeros and coefficients; real coefficients; fractional rational expressions and their canonical form — including the convention, stated in the text (printed p. 99), that a cancelled fraction takes at a cancelled point the value of its reduced form; **partial fractions** (every proper fraction uniquely a sum of a polynomial and a proper fraction, by division — the first step of the decomposition); polynomial equations.
- **Equations and inequalities, second pass (Theme 5).** Exponential, logarithmic and trigonometric equations (the elective treatment, alongside the compulsory book's), systems of two such equations in two unknowns, then the inequality families in the same order: linear and quadratic, systems, fractional rational, exponential, logarithmic, trigonometric.
- **Complex numbers, deepened (Theme 6).** The number-system story is retold as motivation (sight-read p. 153: N adjoin negatives for x + 3 = 2, Z adjoin rationals for 3x = 4, Q adjoin reals for x² = 2; x² = −1 has no real solution, so a new symbol i with i² = −1 is adjoined and ℂ = {a + bi | a, b ∈ R} is defined, with R as the subset a + 0i, Re/Im notation and the conjugate z̄ = a − bi). Then the **trigonometric form** via polar coordinates (r, φ) (unit 6.2, opening sight-read at p. 158, preceded by plane-region tasks on conditions like 1 ≤ |z| ≤ 3 and Re(z) ≥ 0, Im(z) ≥ 0, |z| ≤ 2); multiplication and division in trigonometric form; **De Moivre's formula** (sight-read p. 165: zⁿ = rⁿ(cos nφ + i sin nφ); worked (1 + i)¹² = [√2(cos π/4 + i sin π/4)]¹² = 2⁶(cos 3π + i sin 3π) = −64 — recomputed correct; the formula stated for negative integer exponents too; cos 3φ and sin 3φ then derived by expanding (cos φ + i sin φ)³) and rooting of complex numbers; modulus inequalities |z₁| − |z₂| type bounds for sums and differences; applications of the trigonometric form (the index records "примитивни корени на бројот 1" — primitive roots of the number 1 — at p. 168).

### Honest negatives — what an elective Grade 12 Algebra book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (printed pp. 186–187) contains no entry for any of the following, and none appeared in the sight-read pages. (Contents-based negatives, as above.)

- **No combinatorics or probability** — no counting, no binomial theorem (the binomial formula is in the compulsory Mathematics book, §3.1.5; Pascal's triangle does not appear here).
- **No sequences or progressions**, no mathematical induction as a topic (induction is used inside proofs but is not a unit; the induction unit is in the compulsory book, §3.1.1).
- **No geometry or trigonometry of triangles** — trigonometry enters only as trigonometric equations and inequalities (units 5.3, 5.10) and inside De Moivre applications.
- **No functions-and-graphs strand** — no study of the course of a function, no inverse functions; [x] and {x} are treated as number-theoretic functions, not graphed as a topic.
- **No matrices, determinants or vectors** — linear algebra is entirely in the companion elective book.
- **No calculus** — no limits (a "value at a cancelled point" convention appears in unit 4.11, but no limit concept is defined), no derivatives or integrals.
- **No Cauchy-Schwarz or named inequality suite beyond "some more important inequalities"** (unit 3.5) — the contents names no individual inequality (AM–GM is present through the means unit 3.4 but is not named as such in the contents).

### Confirmed book errors

1. **Printed p. 37 (PDF p. 38), unit 2.5, Теорема 1 — the multiplicativity of Euler's function is stated without its coprimality hypothesis (confirmed by sight-reading the page at 100 dpi and re-checking the statement at 200 dpi).** The theorem is printed: "Ојлеровата функција φ е мултипликативна, т.е. φ(m·n) = φ(m)·φ(n) **за секои m, n ∈ N**" ("for **all** m, n ∈ N"). As printed this is false: the book's own table on the same page gives φ(2) = 1 and φ(4) = 2, but φ(2·2) = φ(4) = 2 ≠ 1 = φ(2)·φ(2). The proof that follows immediately assumes the missing hypothesis ("Нека НЗД(m,n) = 1" — let gcd(m, n) = 1), so the theorem as *proved* is the correct one; only the statement drops the condition. **Teach-corrected wording:** φ is multiplicative **for coprime arguments**: if gcd(m, n) = 1 then φ(m·n) = φ(m)·φ(n) — and not in general (φ(4) ≠ φ(2)φ(2)).
2. No other confirmed errors. All other worked examples and printed results recomputed during the read checked out, including: ∛3 > √2 via ⁶√9 > ⁶√8, √4356 = 66 and √298116 = 546 (printed p. 5); the floor identity [n/4] + [2n/4] + [3n/4] = (3n − 3)/2 for n coprime to 4 (printed p. 25); the table φ(1)…φ(12) (printed p. 37); 11 | 2³⁴⁰ − 1 (printed p. 39); the Bezout remainder 15 obtained both by f(−2) and by long division (printed p. 76); the undetermined-coefficient results (x + 3)(x − 2)(x + 4) = x³ + 5x² − 2x − 24 and the quotient/remainder x² − 2x + 3 / 3x + 2 (printed p. 90); and (1 + i)¹² = −64 (printed p. 165). One apparent slip was checked and is **not** a teaching error: the contents (printed p. 187) calls unit 4.12's target "збир од прости множители" (sum of simple *factors*) where the unit itself correctly works with "прости дропки" (partial *fractions*) — a contents wording slip, recorded here so it is not "corrected" into the unit.

### Held-copy defects

- **Extent mismatch between the CIP and the held copy (the book's own records, flagged for the coordinator).** The colophon's CIP records **184 pages** (Skopje, 2005), but the held copy's printed folios run to **187** (contents, printed pp. 186–187) inside 189 PDF pages (cover + printed 1–188, the colophon being the unnumbered final page, printed 188's verso position — PDF p. 189). The teaching body, answers (p. 177) and index (to p. 185) are all present and every contents entry sight-read sits at its listed page under the +1 offset, so the copy is **complete against its own contents**; the 184-page CIP figure belongs to an earlier state of the edition and was not updated. Cite the extent from the held copy (folios to 187 + colophon), with the CIP figure noted.
- **Every page carries the diagonal publisher watermark "PROSVETNO DELO"** (as in the other two Просветно дело books of this band); it overlays but does not hide the text at 100 dpi.
- The title page prints **no year**; the year carried into citations (2005) is the CIP/colophon year of the recorded edition, and the edition is the IV издание of the title page — the held PDF gives no separate printing date for this fourth edition.

The chapters are summarised: (1) determinants of the second and third order and the determinant method for systems of two and three unknowns; (2) the concept of a vector in space and the operations — addition, subtraction, multiplication by a number, scalar and vector multiplication; (3) analytic geometry in space — points, lines and planes given algebraically and read back geometrically; (4) the structure **vector space** defined and its properties studied; (5) linear mappings and matrices, with Gauss's method praised as universal and untouched ("неприкосновен") in modern mathematics. ### Everything taught (own summary; details from the sight-read pages)

- **Determinants and Cramer systems (Chapter 1).** The second-order determinant is defined as the number ad − bc attached to the scheme |a b; c d|, with the main and secondary diagonals named (sight-read p. 5: worked |−2 5; 3 4| = −2·4 − 5·3 = −23 — recomputed correct; properties: transposing rows/columns does not change the value, two equal rows give 0, swapping rows changes the sign). Systems of two equations in two, then in three, unknowns are solved determinantally. Third-order determinants: the Sarrus expansion a₁b₂c₃ + a₂b₃c₁ + a₃b₁c₂ − a₃b₂c₁ − a₂b₁c₃ − a₁b₃c₂ (sight-read p. 14), cofactor expansion by a row/column, properties; then **Cramer's formulas are proved**, not just stated (sight-read p. 23: multiply the three equations by the cofactors A₁₁, A₂₁, A₃₁… and add, using the expansion and alien-cofactor properties, to reach x₀·Δ = Δx, and similarly y₀·Δ = Δy, z₀·Δ = Δz; the case Δ ≠ 0 gives x₀ = Δx/Δ etc.). Gauss's method for three equations and homogeneous systems close the chapter; a task in the systems unit already asks the pupil to prove that the midpoint of two solutions of a (homogeneous-type) system is again a solution — an early linearity idea.
- **Vectors in space (Chapter 2).** Geometric vectors first: basic concepts, addition (definition and properties), subtraction, scalar multiplication, the **ratio of collinear vectors** (unit 2.5, "делење" of collinear vectors), linear combinations; then coordinates and coordinate operations; orthogonal projection of a vector; the **scalar product** (sight-read p. 63: a·b = |a||b|cos(a, b), with the sign discussion by angle; worked 2·3·cos 60° = 3 and 4·7·cos 120° = −14 — recomputed correct) and its coordinate computation; the **cross product** (definition, properties, coordinates); and the **mixed product** of three vectors with its geometric interpretation (volume) and determinant computation.
- **Analytic geometry in space (Chapter 3).** Point in space, distance between two points, dividing a segment in a given ratio (tasks include midpoints of a triangle's sides and its centroid — тежиште — in space, sight-read p. 86). The **plane**: introduced by the ways it can be determined (three non-collinear points; a normal vector and a distance; a point and a line through it; two intersecting lines), then the **normal equation** in vector form r·n₀ − p = 0 (sight-read p. 86, Theorem 1), general and intercept equations, plane through one point and through three points, distance point–plane, angle between two planes and their orthogonality condition. The **line in space** in its various forms, through two points, distance point–line, angles line–line and line–plane. Mutual positions: two planes, two lines (including skew), line and plane.
- **Vector spaces (Chapter 4).** The abstract turn: R² = {(x, y)} and R³ = {(x, y, z)} are generalised to a **(real) vector space** defined by axioms (sight-read p. 119: the eight axioms are printed in two groups — C₁–C₄ for addition, under which (V, +) is an abelian group, and M₁–M₄ for scalar multiplication, M₄ being 1u = u; the text notes the elements of the field R are called scalars). Subspaces; linear combination and **linear hull (покривка, span)**; sum and direct sum of subspaces; linear dependence/independence; **basis and dimension**; coordinates of a vector in a basis.
- **Matrices and linear mappings (Chapter 5).** Systems in n unknowns first (sight-read p. 139: a solution is an n-tuple u = (u₁, …, uₙ), "interpreted as a vector in Rⁿ"; checked example — u = (3, 2, 1, 0) satisfies x + 2y − 4z + t = 3 since 3 + 2·2 − 4·1 + 0 = 3, while v = (1, 2, 4, 1) gives −10 ≠ 3 — recomputed correct), Gauss's method in general, then **matrices** proper (sight-read p. 150: a matrix is a rectangular scheme A = [aᵢⱼ] with real entries; rows are the horizontal m-tuples, columns the vertical ones; transpose), matrix addition, scalar and matrix multiplication, square matrices. Mappings: the concept of a mapping, **linear mappings**, bijective ones (isomorphisms), **kernel (јадро) and image**, operations on mappings, and the **matrix representation** of a linear mapping. The matrix–system machinery closes the book: matrix form of a system, echelon matrices, row equivalence and elementary row transformations, row space, **rank** — with the theorem that row rank and column rank are equal **proved** (sight-read p. 185) — the rank's role for systems (sight-read p. 190: a rank-2 homogeneous system in 5 unknowns has a 3-dimensional solution space with the explicit basis v₁ = (−2, 1, 0, 0, 0), v₂ = (−1, 0, ½, 1, 0), v₃ = (−3, 0, −1, 0, 1) — all three vectors re-substituted and verified as solutions, and independent), the **inverse matrix** (uniqueness proved: from AB₁ = B₁A = I and AB₂ = B₂A = I follows B₁ = B₁I = B₁(AB₂) = (B₁A)B₂ = IB₂ = B₂ — sight-read p. 190), and elementary transformations applied to find it.

### Honest negatives — what an elective Grade 12 linear-algebra book might teach that this one does NOT

Basis: the book's own complete contents (printed pp. 223–225) contains no entry for any of the following, and none appeared in the sight-read pages. (Contents-based negatives, as above.)

- **No determinants above order 3, and no general determinant theory** — determinants stop at the third order; the inverse matrix is found by elementary transformations (unit 5.20), not by the adjugate/determinant formula, and no unit treats det(AB) or the determinant of a general square matrix.
- **No eigenvalues or eigenvectors**, no diagonalisation, no characteristic polynomial — nowhere in the contents.
- **No inner-product spaces as an abstract structure** — the scalar product is taught for geometric/coordinate vectors (Chapter 2); Chapter 4's vector spaces carry no inner product, norm-as-axiom, Gram–Schmidt or orthogonal bases.
- **No change-of-basis / transition matrices** — coordinates in a basis are taught (unit 4.7) and the matrix representation of a mapping (unit 5.12), but no unit treats changing the basis or similar matrices.
- **No linear programming, no conic sections or quadrics in space** — analytic geometry stops at points, lines and planes in space (the conics are in the compulsory Mathematics book, and as plane curves).
- **No applications chapter** — no cryptography, Markov chains, Leontief or computer-graphics applications are named in the contents; applications stay inside exercises (e.g. centroid tasks, unit 3.1).

### Confirmed book errors

**None found in the pages sight-read.** Every worked computation on the sight-read pages was recomputed and is correct, including: |−2 5; 3 4| = −23 (printed p. 5); the Sarrus expansion terms for the third-order determinant (printed p. 14); the scalar products 2·3·cos 60° = 3 and 4·7·cos 120° = −14 (printed p. 63); the solution checks u = (3, 2, 1, 0) (value 3) and v = (1, 2, 4, 1) (value −10 ≠ 3) for x + 2y − 4z + t = 3 (printed p. 139); the solution-space basis v₁, v₂, v₃ of the rank example (printed p. 190) — each re-substituted into both independent equations x + 2y − 4z + 3r − s = 0 and 2z − r + 2s = 0 and verified; and the printed answers sight-read at printed p. 203 (1.3.1 task 2: x₁ = 3, x₂ = 2). Because the read was contents + front/back matter + the sight-read lesson pages named above, this is "none found in the pages sight-read", not a guarantee about every exercise on all 226 pages.

### Held-copy defects

- **CIP year vs printing (the book's own, flagged for citation care):** the colophon's CIP records the edition as Skopje **2007** (226 pp., ISBN 9989-0-0386-6), while the title page dates this IV издание's printing **Скопје, 2017** and the colophon states the manuscript went to print in November 2017 and printing finished in December 2017. The CIP block was carried over from the 2007 edition; cite the year as **2017 (IV издание)** with the CIP data noted.
- **Every page carries the diagonal publisher watermark "PROSVETNO DELO"** (as in the other two Просветно дело books of this band); it overlays but does not hide the text at 100 dpi.
- Otherwise complete and consistent: the colophon extent (226 pages) matches the offset arithmetic exactly (227 PDF pages = cover + printed 1–226); contents, answers (from p. 203) are present at the back where this book keeps them; no missing or duplicated folios were found in the pages checked, and every contents-listed unit start sight-read sits at its listed page under the +1 offset.

*Citation for this book: North Macedonia + Линеарна алгебра и аналитичка геометрија за трета година на реформираното гимназиско образование (изборен предмет) + Grade 12 (gymnasium year III) + page + 2017 (IV издание; CIP 2007).*
